Non-abelian Cohen–Lenstra heuristics in the presence of roots of unity
Yuan Liu

Abstract
. For a Galois extension K / 𝔽 q ​ ( t ) K/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t) of Galois group Γ \Gamma with gcd ⁡ ( q , | Γ | ) = 1 \gcd(q,|\Gamma|)=1 , we define an invariant ω K \omega_{K} , and show that it determines the Weil pairing of the curve corresponding to K K and it descends to the prime-to- | Γ | |\Gamma| -torsion part of the lifting invariants of Hurwitz schemes introduced by Ellenberg–Venkatesh–Westerland and Wood. By keeping track of the image of ω K \omega_{K} , we compute, as K K varies and q → ∞ q\to\infty , the average number of surjections from the Galois group of maximal unramified extension of K K to H H , for any Γ \Gamma -group H H whose order is prime to q ​ | Γ | q|\Gamma| . Motivated by this result, we modify the conjecture of Wood, Zureick-Brown and the author about non-abelian Cohen–Lenstra, for both function fields and number fields, to cover the cases when the base field contains extra roots of unity. We also discuss how to use the invariant ω K \omega_{K} to construct a random group model, and prove in a special case that the model produces the same moments as our function field result.



1.
Introduction

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group. The Cohen–Lenstra
[
8
]
and Cohen–Martinet
[
9
]
heuristics give predictions for the distribution of the class groups of
K
as
K
varies over all
\Gamma
-extensions of
{\mathbb{Q}}
with prescribed signature. Although only few cases of these heuristics are proven, huge breakthroughs have been made in the function field analogue, which was first formulated by Friedman and Washington
[
13
]
. Ellenberg, Venkatesh, and Westerland
[
12
]
translated the heuristics into questions about counting the
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points of the Hurwitz schemes, the moduli schemes of branched covers of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, and proved a version (as
q\to\infty
) of the function field analogue of the Cohen–Lenstra heuristics.

The class group is the Galois group of the maximal abelian unramified extension. So it is natural to study the distributions of the non-abelian analogues of the class group. In
[
4
]
and
[
5
]
, Boston, Bush, and Hajir extended the Cohen–Lenstra heuristics to a pro-
\ell
setting (for an odd prime
\ell
) by considering the distribution of the Galois groups of the maximal unramified pro-
\ell
extensions of quadratic fields. Boston and Wood
[
7
]
proved the function field analogue of the Boston–Bush–Hajir heuristics. Wood, Zureick-Brown and the author
[
21
]
considered a more generalized setting. Let
Q
be a global field that is either
{\mathbb{Q}}
or
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
. As
K
varies over all
\Gamma
-extensions of
Q
that is split completely at
\infty
,
[
21
]
gives conjectures of the distribution of the Galois group of the maximal unramified extension of
K
whose degree is prime to both
|\Gamma|
and the number of roots of unity in
Q
, and not divisible by
\Char(Q)
; and also proves the function field analogue as
q\to\infty
.

All of the conjectures above fail when the base field contains extra roots of unity. For example, the Cohen–Lenstra–Martinet heuristics predict that the probability that a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-3})
(which contains the
3
rd roots of unity) has trivial
3
-part of class group is
\prod_{i=2}^{\infty}(1-3^{-i})\approx.840
, however, Malle’s numerical result
[
25
]
suggests that this probability should be
\frac{4}{3}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}(1+3^{-i})^{-1}\approx.852
. The reason for this phenomenon is better understood in the function field case: Friedman and Washington in
[
13
]
already discussed the importance of the Weil pairing of the curve corresponding to the function field extension
K/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
. When the
\ell
-th roots of unity
\mu_{\ell}
is contained in
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
(that is, when
\ell\mid q-1
), the Weil pairing reduces to some non-trivial structure on the
\ell
-part of the class group of
K
, which makes the distribution of class groups different from the the case that
\mu_{\ell}\not\subset{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
. By keeping track of the image of the Weil pairing, Lipnowski and Tsimerman
[
20
]
refined the Cohen–Lenstra heuristics for the distribution of the
\ell
-part of class groups of quadratic extensions over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
in the case when
\ell\mid q-1
, and used the results of Ellenberg–Venkatesh–Westerland to prove the refined conjecture as
q\to\infty
; and Lipnowski, Sawin, and Tsimerman
[
19
]
gave a similar conjecture in the number field case, by making local adjustments at the primes lying over
\ell
and
\infty
.

In this paper, we generalize the non-abelian analogues of Cohen–Lenstra heurisitics to include the roots-of-unity case. Let
\Gamma
be a finite group, and
Q
be a number field or
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
.
As
K
varies over
\Gamma
-extensions of
Q
that is split completely at each archimedean prime when
Q
is a number field or at
\infty
when
Q={\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
, we study the distribution of the Galois group of the maximal unramified and split-completely-at-
\infty
extension of
K
whose degree is prime to
|\Gamma|
and not divisible by
\Char(Q)
.

1.1.
Main results

The bulk of this paper deals with the function field case, that is
Q={\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
. A
\Gamma
-extension
K/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
that is split completely at
\infty
corresponds to a
\Gamma
-cover
X/{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
.
By passing this extension to
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, we first define an invariant
\omega_{K}
associated to
K
that, roughly speaking, plays the role of the Weil pairing associated to
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
in the non-abelian setting. For a field
L
, we let
G_{\O}(L):=L_{\O}/L
denote the Galois group of the maximal unramified extension
L_{\O}
of
L
. Consider the exact sequence

1\longrightarrow G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})\longrightarrow G_{\O}(K)\longrightarrow\Gal(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}/K)=\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\Frob\longrightarrow 1,

and a splitting
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\Frob\to G_{\O}(K)
defined by identifying
\Frob
with the Frobenius element in the decomposition group of the extension
K_{\O}/K
at a preselected place of
K_{\O}
over
\infty
. Then this splitting defines a
\Frob
action on
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
by conjugation. We let
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
denote the pro-prime-to-
q|\Gamma|
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
and denote the corresponding extension by
K^{\prime}_{\O}/K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t)
. (Equivalently,
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
is the pro-prime-to-
q|\Gamma|
completion of the étale fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})
.) Then
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
naturally obtains an action of
\Frob
from
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
; and similarly, we can define a
\Frob
action on the Galois group
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t))
.
Moreover,
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
also has an action of
\Gamma
by conjugation when we fix a homomorphic splitting of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t))\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t)/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t))\simeq\Gamma
.
We define the invariant
\omega_{K}
to be a specific
\Frob
-equivariant isomorphism (see Definition
2.8
)

\omega_{K}:\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}

where the subscript
(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
represents the pro-prime-to-
q|\Gamma|
completion. The invariant
\omega_{K}
is first defined group-theoretically by studying the Schur covering group of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t))
. Then we also give a cohomological definition:
\omega_{K}
defines a central extension of
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
by
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, and hence corresponds to a class in
H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})
; under the functorial isomorphism between Galois cohomology and étale cohomology, the corresponding class in
H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})
has trace
-|\Gamma|
(see Lemma
2.11
and Corollary
2.12
).

Then we pull everything back to
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
by taking the maximal
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient fixed by the
\Frob
action. Such quotient of
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
is naturally the Galois group of the maximal unramified extension of
K
that is splitting completely over
\infty
and has degree prime-to-
(q|\Gamma|)
, and we denote this quotient by
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
and the corresponding extension by
K^{\#}_{\O}/K
. For a subextension
L
of
K^{\#}_{\O}/K
that is Galois over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
, we define an invariant
\omega_{L/K}
to be the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{L/K}:\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}(\Gal(L/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coinf}

where
\coinf
is the coinflation map defined by the quotient map between Galois groups. All the maps are
\Frob
-equivariant and
\Frob
acts trivially on the last term, so
\omega_{L/K}
factors through
\mu_{q-1}
, and
\im\omega_{L/K}
is contained in the
(q-1)
-torsion subgroup of
H_{2}(\Gal(L/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
.

The invariant
\omega_{K}
is “abelianized” to the Weil pairing associated to
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
. Let
\ell
be a prime number such that
\ell\nmid q|\Gamma|
. The pro-
\ell
completion
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})(\ell)
of
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
is a pro-
\ell
Demuškin group (a special type of one-relator pro-
\ell
group). Similarly to what we discussed previously, the invariant
\omega_{K}
defines to a central extension of
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})(\ell)
by
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)
, from which we obtain information of the relator in a pro-
\ell
presentation of
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})(\ell)
. This information, by properties of Demuškin groups (namely, the transgression map associated to the presentation), completely determines the cup product

H^{1}(G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\times H^{1}(G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\overset{\cup}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).

Because
G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
acts trivially on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)
and
H^{1}(G_{\O}(K\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))
can be identified as the
\ell
-adic Tate module of
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
, the cup product above followed by the trace map gives the Weil pairing associated to
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
. Similarly, the invariant
\omega_{L/K}
determines the image of the Weil pairing (see Proposition
3.6
, and also see Definition
3.5
for the definition of the image of Weil pairing).

Then, we generalize the results of Lipnowski–Sawin–Tsimerman to the non-abelian setting by keeping track of our invariant
\omega_{L/K}
. In fact,
\omega_{K}
is defined in the way such that
\omega_{L/K}
agrees with the prime-to-
|\Gamma|
-torsion part of a component invariant of Hurwitz schemes, which is first introduced by Ellenberg–Venkatesh–Westerland and then carefully explained by Wood in
[
37
]
(we refer this invariant by the
EVW-W lifting invariant
). Then by modifying the moment computation using Hurwitz schemes in
[
21
]
, we obtain the moment result in the function field case as
q\to\infty
. Let
\rDisc K
denote the norm of the radical of the ideal
\Disc(K/Q)
. Let
E_{\Gamma}(D,Q)
be the set of the pairs
(K,\iota)
, where
K
is a split-completely-at-
\infty
extension of
Q
with
\rDisc K=D
, and
\iota
is an isomorphism
\Gal(K/Q)\to\Gamma
. An
admissible
\Gamma
-group
is a group with a
\Gamma
action such that it is of order prime to
|\Gamma|
and is generated, under the action of
\Gamma
, by the elements in the form of
g^{-1}\gamma(g)
for
g\in G,\,\gamma\in\Gamma
.
All
\Gamma
-equivariant quotients of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
are admissible
\Gamma
-groups.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group,
H
a finite admissible
\Gamma
-group, and
\delta
a group homomorphism
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. Then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\to\infty\\
(q,|\Gamma||H|)=1\\
\ord(\im\delta)\mid q-1\end{subarray}}\dfrac{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\sum\limits_{K\in E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}\left(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H\right)\,\Bigl\rvert\,\pi_{*}\circ\omega_{K^{\#}/K}=\delta\right\}}{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\#E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]},

where in the limit
q
is always a prime power,
\ord(\im\delta)
is the order of the image of
\delta
, and
\pi_{*}
is the coinflation map
H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\#}_{\O}/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
induced by
\pi
.

Inspired by this theorem, we make the following conjecture about the distribution of the prime-to-
\Char(Q)|\Gamma|
completion of the maximal unramified Galois group of
K
, for a fixed base field
Q
, in both the function field case and the number field case.

Conjecture 1.2

(Moment part of Conjecture
6.4
, and Conjecture
6.7
)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group,
H
a finite admissible
\Gamma
-group. Let
q
be a prime power such that
\gcd(q,|\Gamma||H|)=1
, and
\delta
a group homomorphism
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. If
\ord(\im\delta)\mid q-1
, then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\dfrac{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\sum\limits_{K\in E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}\left(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H\right)\,\Bigl\rvert\,\pi_{*}\circ\omega_{K^{\#}/K}=\delta\right\}}{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\#E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]};

otherwise,
\#\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H)\mid\pi_{*}\circ\omega_{K^{\#}/K}=\delta\}=0
for any
K\in E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))
.

Let
Q
be a number field, and
m
the maximal integer such that the
m
-th roots of unity is contained in
Q
. For any extension
K/Q
, we define
G^{\#}_{\O}(K):=G_{\O}(K)_{(|\Gamma||G_{\O}(Q)|)^{\prime}}
.
Assume that
G_{\O}(Q)
is pro-prime-to
|H|
.
Then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\sum\limits_{D\leq N}\sum\limits_{K\in E_{\Gamma}(D,Q)}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}\left(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H\right)}{\sum\limits_{D\leq N}\#E_{\Gamma}(D,Q)}=\frac{\#H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[m]}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]\cdot|H|^{u}},

where
u
is the rank of the group of units in the ring
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}
of algebraic integers of
Q
.

We add the requirement “pro-prime-to-
|G_{\O}(Q)|
” in the number field conjecture because, when
G_{\O}(Q)\neq 1
, the group
G_{\O}(K)_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
is not an admissible
\Gamma
-group and its distribution would be very different from the function field case.

We also conjecture that there exists a unique probability measure that gives the conjectural moment in the function field case (see Conjecture
6.1
). Then the conjectural moment in the number field case is obtained by taking the quotient of a group according to this probability measure modulo
u
elements according to the Haar measure. This number field conjecture is inspired by
[
26
]
,
[
19
]
, and two other evidences from algebraic number theory:

(1)

Using the previous work
[
23
]
of the author, we show that, in a presentation of the Galois group
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
, it needs
u
more relators in the number field case than in the function field case. (See §
6.2
)

(2)

When
Q={\mathbb{Q}}(\mu_{\ell})
and
K
is nice (namely, when
K
is
\ell
-CM), Schmidt
[
33
]
proved that there is a maximal
positively ramified
pro-
\ell
extension
K_{\pos}(\ell)
of
K
such that the Galois group of
K_{\pos}(\ell)
over the cyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-extension
K_{\infty}
of
K
is a very nice analogue of the pro-
\ell
completion of the étale fundamental group of
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
. Using this positively ramified extension, we show that
G_{\O}(K)(\ell)
is the quotient of
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
modulo
u
elements, where
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
is the maximal quotient of
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/K_{\infty})
fixed by
\Gal(K_{\infty}/K)
(which is the analogue of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
from the function field case). (See §
6.3
)

Regarding the function field case,
another advantage of studying the invariant
\omega_{K}
is that it shows us how to construct a random group model to predict the distribution of interest to us.
By the Grothendieck’s theory of specialization,
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
has a pro-prime-to-
q|\Gamma|
presentation in the form of
\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{2g}\mid[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2g-1},x_{2g}]\rangle
, where
g
is the genus of
X_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
. Let
F
be the free pro-prime-to-
q|\Gamma|
group on generators
x_{1},\ldots,x_{2g}
, and let
R
be the closed normal subgroup of
F
generated by
[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2g-1},x_{2g}]
. Then the short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow R/[F,R]\longrightarrow F/[F,R]\longrightarrow G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)\longrightarrow 1

(1.1)

is a central extension with
R/[F,R]\simeq\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
; and moreover, the kernel is contained in the commutator of
F/[F,R]
, so the extension is stem. Note that
H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})
is the Schur multiplier of
G
, and it is isomorphic to the kernel of a Schur covering (that is, a maximal stem extension) of
G
. Thus, the isomorphism
\omega_{K}
implies that there are an action of
\Frob
and an action of
\Gamma
on the sequence (
1.1
) such that they preserve the kernel
R/[F,R]
, they induce the natural
\Frob
and
\Gamma
actions on
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
, and moreover, on
R/[F,R]
,
\Gamma
acts trivially and
\Frob
acts as multiplication by
q
. Then we expect that by considering the
\Frob
action as a random automorphism of
F/[F,R]
satisfying all the requirements, we can obtain a random group model that gives the conjectural moment in the function field case.

In general, understanding the group structure of such random group models is difficult.
In this paper, we only study this random group model when we focus on the pro-
\ell
completions for an odd prime
\ell
and
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
. By the classification of Demuškin group by Labute
[
18
]
, a pro-
\ell
Demuškin group whose abelianization is torsion-free necessarily has a presentation as described in the preceding paragraph, and we denote such Demuškin group by
{\mathcal{G}}_{g}
. Then, up to isomorphism,
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
admits a unique Schur covering

1\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\overset{\pi_{n}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1,

and we show that there exists a
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
action (unique up to isomorphism) on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
such that the action preserves and acts trivially on
\ker\pi_{n}
and its induced action on the abelianization of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is taking inversion (the last condition is naturally satisfied by the
\Gamma
action on
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
, since the étale fundamental group of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
is trivial). Then we define a random group
Y_{q,n}
to be the maximal
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
fixed by a random automorphism of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that preserves and acts as multiplication by
q
on
\ker\pi_{n}
. (See Definition
5.3
for a more careful definition of the random group.) Then we prove that the moment obtained by
Y_{q,n}
is exactly the moment in Conjecture
1.2
.

Theorem 1.3

(see Theorem
5.6
)
.

Let
H
be a finite
\ell
-group with an action of
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
H
is admissible. Let
n
be a sufficiently large integer such that there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant surjection from
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
to
H
. Let
\delta
be a group homomorphism
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)
such that
\ord(\im\delta)\mid q-1
. Then

{\mathbb{E}}(\#\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(Y_{q,n},H)\mid\pi_{\dagger}=\delta\})=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]},

where
\pi_{\dagger}
is the coinflation map
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\simeq H_{2}({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)
induced by the
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient map
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\to Y_{q,n}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}H
.

We conjecture that, as
n\to\infty
, the probability measures defined by
Y_{q,n}
weakly converge to a probability measure that gives the moments in Conjecture
1.2
(see Conjecture
6.3
). Theorem
1.3
is proved by studing the orbits of
\Sur_{\Gamma}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)
under the action of the automorphisms of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that preserves and acts as multiplication by
q
on
\ker\pi_{n}
. By using the method of successive approximation in
[
18
]
, we prove that two surjections in
\Sur_{\Gamma}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)
are in the same orbit if and only if they induce the same coinflation map. This random group model can also be used in other scenarios with similar group structures. For example, in Appendix
A
, we apply the model to study the fundamental groups of random 3-manifolds constructed by random Heegaard splittings, in the notion defined by Dunfield and Thurston
[
11
]
. We show that when
\phi
is a random automorphism of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
(forgetting the
\Gamma
action) that preserves and acts trivially on
\ker\pi_{n}
, the moment obtained by the random group

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\displaystyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{[x_{1},x_{3},\ldots,x_{2n-1},\phi(x_{1}),\phi(x_{3}),\ldots,\phi(x_{2n-1})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\textstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{[x_{1},x_{3},\ldots,x_{2n-1},\phi(x_{1}),\phi(x_{3}),\ldots,\phi(x_{2n-1})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{[x_{1},x_{3},\ldots,x_{2n-1},\phi(x_{1}),\phi(x_{3}),\ldots,\phi(x_{2n-1})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{[x_{1},x_{3},\ldots,x_{2n-1},\phi(x_{1}),\phi(x_{3}),\ldots,\phi(x_{2n-1})]}$}}}

agrees with the average size of
\Sur(\pi_{1}(M),H)
, for any
\ell
-group
H
, as
M
varies as a random 3-manifold (see Proposition
A.1
).

1.2.
Prior works and further questions

The study of random group models that are relating to the Cohen–Lenstra type of questions started at the very beginning: in the original paper
[
8
]
of Cohen and Lenstra, they used the random matrix

\lim_{n\to\infty}\coker(M),\quad\text{ for a random $n\times(n+1)$ matrix $M$ with ${\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}$-entries},

to give the probability measure in the totally real case of their conjecture. Translating it into a random abelian group, this model is equivalent to

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\oplus n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.47221pt}{$\displaystyle{\text{$n+1$ random elements}}$}}{\raisebox{3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\oplus n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.47221pt}{$\textstyle{\text{$n+1$ random elements}}$}}{\raisebox{3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\oplus n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.43054pt}{$\scriptstyle{\text{$n+1$ random elements}}$}}{\raisebox{2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\oplus n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.7361pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{$n+1$ random elements}}$}}}.

In all the generalizations of Cohen–Lenstra in the non-roots-of-unity case, the corresponding random group models are constructed using balanced presentations (that is, presentations such that the difference between the numbers of generators and relators is fixed). Boston–Bush–Hajir studied the balanced pro-
\ell
presentations in
[
4
,
5
]
. Wood, Zureick-Brown and the author in
[
21
]
gave the conjectural probability measures by using a form of balanced presentations of admissible
\Gamma
-groups, which is a modification of the probability measures of random balanced presentations studied in
[
22
]
. In the non-roots-of-unity case, the random balanced presentation models work very well, and the author proved in
[
23
]
that the unramified Galois groups of interest to us can always be obtained by a balanced
\Gamma
-presentation as conjectured in
[
21
]
.

However, when considering the roots-of-unity case, the balanced presentation models fail.
Similar phenomenon happens to random 3-manifolds: Dunfield and Thurston in
[
11
]
tried to use balanced presentations to study random 3-manifolds, but they concluded that the fundamental groups of random 3-manifolds have many more finite quotients than the random balanced presentation model. It was first suggested by Friedman and Washington
[
13
]
that, instead of studying random presentation, we could consider the Frobenius map as a random automorphism acting on Jacobian varieties, and hence study the random matrix

\lim_{n\to\infty}\coker(I_{n}-{\mathcal{F}})

(1.2)

where
{\mathcal{F}}
is a random invertible matrix that is compatible with the Weil pairing on Jacobian. The random matrices in this setting and their relationship with function field case have been vastly studied, for example, see
[
17
,
1
,
2
,
14
,
3
,
20
,
19
]
.

The random group model constructed in this paper is a non-abelian generalization of (
1.2
) and sets up a framework for the study of the non-abelian Cohen–Lenstra heuristics in the roots of unity case. There are many important questions in this direct that are left to be solved. We don’t know how to compute the probability measure defined by
Y_{q,n}
, and whether the limit of the measure as
n\to\infty
has total mass 1. Although, Theorem
1.3
strongly suggests that our random group model is the right object to study, what we really want is that the moment with respect to the limit measure equals to
1/[H:H^{\Gamma}]
(that is, to take
\lim_{n\to\infty}
inside
{\mathbb{E}}
). The recent work of Sawin and Wood
[
30
,
31
]
studies, in a very general setting, how one can get the probability measure directly from the moments, without the help of any random group structures; and we expect that their idea can be applied to our moment results and to prove some of the conjectures in Section
6
.

Also, the moment result for
Y_{q,n}
in Theorem
1.3
cannot be easily generalized to the situation either when
\Gamma\not\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
, or when studying the distribution of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
, not just of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
. The main reasons of this difficulty are: 1) when
\Gamma\not\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
, we don’t know how to give a canonical definition of a pro-
\ell
Demuškin
\Gamma
-group as in Lemma
5.2
; and 2) the proof of Theorem
1.3
vastly uses the filtration of pro-
\ell
groups given by the lower central series, which does not exists for a general group.

There is a potential to apply our random group model to obtain some numerical results. For example, for fixed
n
and
q
, we can take the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion of the random group
Y_{q,n}
for a finite set of
\ell
-groups
{\mathcal{C}}
(for the definition of pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion, see §
6.1
). Since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
is always finite, it is possible to list all the automorphisms of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfying the conditions in the definition of
Y_{q,n}
, and then we could see which quotients of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
can occur as
Y_{q,n}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
, and compute the probability that they occur. As
n\to\infty
, if the probability measures converge fast, then we may get an approximation of the probability that
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)(\ell)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq H
for small
\ell
-groups
H
.

For Cohen–Lenstra in the presence of roots of unity, Lipnowski–Sawin–Tsimerman in
[
20
,
19
]
made moment conjectures for the distributions of class groups of quadratic fields. In the non-abelian setting, Wood in
[
36
]
gave a conjecture for the average number of unramified
G
-extensions of a quadratic extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}
or
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
for any finite group
G
. The conjectures made in this paper agree with all the previous conjectures about moments. Wood’s work also considers some cases that cannot be addressed by the method in this paper – it also deals with the case that
G
is of even order and the case that the quadratic fields are imaginary.
Malle in
[
26
]
and Boston–Bush in
[
6
]
gave conjectures and numerical evidences in some cases for the probability measures of the distributions of class groups and class tower groups respectively.

It is also interesting to understand the difference between the function field case and the number field case, which is related to the very important question: for number fields, what is the best analogue of the étale fundamental group from the function field case?
Conjecture
1.2
and the two evidences §
6.2
and §
6.3
strongly suggest that, for any
\Gamma
-extension
K/Q
such that no archimedean prime is ramified, there is some group associated to
K
such that its group structure is similar to the prime-to-characteristic part of the étale fundamental groups of curves and
G_{\O}(K)_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
is the
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of this group modulo
\rank{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}^{\times}
elements. When
K
and
Q
are nice, this group can be defined by the positively ramified extension by
[
33
]
and the discussion in §
6.3
. We would like to understand how to generalize the notion of positively ramifications for arbitrary
K/Q
. When we have a better understanding of this mysterious group, we want to construct an invariant associated to
K
that is analogous to
\omega_{K}
in the function field case, and then we want to know whether this invariant agrees with the invariants defined in previous work (the lifting invariant for number fields in
[
36
]
and the Artin–Verdier pairing invariant in
[
19
]
).

1.3.
Outline of the paper

In Section
2
, we define the invariants
\omega_{K}
and
\omega_{L/K}
: we give the group theoretical definition of
\omega_{K}
in §
2.1
, the cohomological definition of
\omega_{K}
in §
2.2
, and the definition and a group theoretical description of
\omega_{L/K}
in §
2.3
. In Section
3
, we explain how the invariant
\omega_{K}
is related to the Weil pairing in §
3.1
and how
\omega_{L/K}
determines the image of the Weil pairing in §
3.2
. In Section
4
, we first recall the definition of the EVW-W lifting invariants in §
4.1
, we show that
\omega_{L/K}
is the prime-to-
|\Gamma|
-torsion part of the EVW-W lifting invariant in §
4.2
, and we give the proof Theorem
1.1
in §
4.3
. In Section
5
, we construct our random group model in §
5.1
, and give the proof of Theorem
1.3
in §
5.2
and §
5.3
. In Section
6
, we give the statements of conjectures regarding the probability measures and the Cohen–Lenstra moments in §
6.1
, and provide the algebraic number theoretical evidences in §
6.2
and §
6.3
to support Conjecture
1.2
.

1.4.
Notation

For a group
G
and an element
g\in G
, we let
\ord(G)
and
\ord(g)
denote the orders of
G
and
g
respectively. For
a,b\in G
, we write
[a,b]=a^{-1}b^{-1}ab
and
a^{b}=b^{-1}ab
. Whenever we talk about homomorphism of profinite groups, we always mean continuous homomorphisms. A
\Gamma
-group
H
is a profinite group
H
with a continuous action by
\Gamma
, and we write
H\rtimes\Gamma
for the semidirect product defined by the
\Gamma
action on
H
. We write
\Hom_{G}
,
\Sur_{G}
, and
\Aut_{G}
to represent the sets of
G
-equivariant homomorphisms, surjections, and automorphisms respectively. A subgroup of a
\Gamma
-group that is closed under
\Gamma
action is called a
\Gamma
-subgroup, and a
\Gamma
-quotient is the quotient by a normal
\Gamma
-subgroup. We write
G^{\Gamma}
for the maximal
\Gamma
-subgroup of
G
on which
\Gamma
acts trivially. For a profinite group
G
, we write
G({\ell})
and
G_{(n)^{\prime}}
for the pro-
\ell
completion and the pro-prime-to-
n
completion of
G
, and for the latter, we sometimes also call it “pro-
(n)^{\prime}
completion”. A
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-group is a profinite
\Gamma
-group whose finite quotients all have order prime to
|\Gamma|
.

A stem extension of
G
is a central extension of
\pi:\widetilde{G}\twoheadrightarrow G
such that
\ker\pi\subset[\widetilde{G},\widetilde{G}]
. A
Schur covering
of the group
G
is a stem extension of
G
of maximal possible order; or equivalently, a central extension
\pi:\widetilde{G}\to G
such that under the universal coefficients theorem map
H^{2}(G,\ker\pi)\to\Hom(H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}),\ker\pi)
the image of the class of
\pi
is an isomorphism.
A Schur covering always exists, but is not necessarily unique. A
\ell
-Schur covering (resp. a
(n)^{\prime}
-Schur covering) of
G
is a stem extension such that the image of the class of
\pi
maps to a quotient map
H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})\to\ker\pi
and
\ker\pi\simeq H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)
(resp.
\ker\pi\simeq H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(n)^{\prime}}
). Two extensions
\pi_{i}:G_{i}\twoheadrightarrow G
,
i=1,2
are called isomorphic if there exists an isomorphism
\phi:G_{1}\to G_{2}
such that
\pi_{2}\circ\phi=\pi_{1}
.
A splitting of a quotient map
\pi:\widetilde{G}\to G
is a (not necessarily homomorphic) map
s:G\to\widetilde{G}
such that
\pi\circ s
is the identity map.
For a global field
K
and a set
S
of primes of
K
, we write
G_{S}(K)
for the Galois group of the maximal unramified-outside-
S
extension of
K
, and write
K_{S}/K
for the corresponding field extension.

2.
Definition of the invariants

2.1.
Group theoretical definition of the invariant
\omega_{K}

Let
K
be a global field of
\Char(K)=p>0
and let
k\subset K
be the finite constant field with algebraic closure
\overline{k}
. Assume that
K/k(t)
is split completely at the place
\infty
. Then considering an exact sequence

1\longrightarrow G_{\O}(K\overline{k})\longrightarrow G_{\O}(K)\longrightarrow\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))=\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\Frob_{k}\longrightarrow 1,

(2.1)

where
\Frob_{k}
is the Frobenius element of
k
, there is a homomorphic splitting
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}\cdot\Frob_{k}\to G_{\O}(K)
defined by identifying
\Frob_{k}
with the Frobenious element in a decomposition group of
K_{\O}/K
at a preselected place of
K
over
\infty
. Then this splitting defines a
\Frob_{k}
action on
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
by conjugation in
G_{\O}(K)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group, and we further assume that
K
is a Galois extension of
k(x)
with Galois group isomorphic to
\Gamma
. Then
\Gal(K_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
acts on its normal subgroup
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
by conjugation, and therefore
\Gal(K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t))\simeq\Gamma
naturally gives an outer action on
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
.
We let
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
denote the pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
. Then the outer action of
\Gamma
on
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
lifts to a
\Gamma
action on
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. This lift is not unique, but by the Schur–Zassenhaus theorem, all lifts are conjugate, and thus we pick one such lift. Note that
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
is the quotient of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
modulo a characteristic normal subgroup, so it has a
\Frob_{k}
action induced from (
2.1
).

Throughout this section, we use the following notation.

Notation 2.1
.

•

Let
k
be a finite field of
\Char(k)=p>0
,
\Gamma
a non-trivial finite group of order not divisible by
p
, and
K/k(t)
a
\Gamma
-extension that is split completely at
\infty
.

•

Let
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
denote the pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
, and
K^{\prime}_{\O}
denote the corresponding field extension of
K\overline{k}
. We’ve seen that
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
has the actions of
\Frob_{k}
and
\Gamma
.

•

Let
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
denote the maximal
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
such that the induced
\Frob_{k}
action on it is trivial. Then
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is naturally a quotient of
G_{\O}(K)
by (
2.1
), and we let
K^{\#}_{\O}/K
denote the corresponding extension. Equivalently,
K^{\#}_{\O}
is the maximal unramified extension of
K
, that is split completely at all places of
K
over
\infty
and of order prime to
|\Gamma|
and
p
.

•

Similarly, for a prime
\ell
that is prime to
p|\Gamma|
,
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
is the pro-
\ell
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
, and we let
K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)
denote the corresponding field extension of
K\overline{k}
. The pro-
\ell
completion
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is the maximal
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
such that the induced
\Frob_{k}
action on it is trivial, and we let
K^{\#}_{\O}(\ell)/K
denote the corresponding extension.

Then the quotient maps in Notation
2.1
define a
\Gamma
-equivariant surjection
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K^{\#}_{\O}/K)
, and hence we obtain the following group surjection

\rho_{K}:\Gal(K_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K^{\#}_{\O}/k(t))\simeq\Gal(K^{\#}_{\O}/K)\rtimes\Gamma.

(2.2)

Lemma 2.2
.

There is a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}

(2.3)

for any prime
\ell\nmid p|\Gamma|
;
and there is a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}.

(2.4)

As a consequence, there are
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphisms

H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)\quad\text{and}\quad H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}

(2.5)

Proof.

Denote the smooth projective curve over
k
associated to
K
by
X
and let
X_{\overline{k}}
denote the base change of
X
to
\overline{k}
. The Galois group
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
is exactly the étale fundamental groups
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X_{\overline{k}})
. Because we assume that
\Gamma
is non-trivial, so
X
has positive genus, then by
[
28
, Remark, page 607]
, the group cohomology of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
agrees with the étale cohomology of
X_{\overline{k}}
. Then by the Poincaré duality, for a prime
\ell\nmid p|\Gamma|
and a positive integer
n
, we have a functorial isomorphism

H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{\ell^{n}})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}},

(2.6)

where both sides have trivial actions of
\Frob_{k}
and
\Gamma
.

We consider the inflation map
\iota_{1}:H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{\ell^{n}})\to H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{\ell^{n}})
. Because
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
is the pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
, we have
H^{1}(\ker(G_{\O}(K\overline{k})\to G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)),\mu_{\ell^{n}})=0
, and hence, it follows by the Hochschild–Serre exact sequence that
\iota_{1}
is an injection. Similary, the inflation map
\iota_{2}:H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell),\mu_{\ell^{n}})\to H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{\ell^{n}})
is an injection. Note that
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
is a pro-
\ell
Demuškin group with free abelianization
[
28
, Thm.10.1.2(ii)]
. Then, because
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell)
acts trivially on
\mu_{\ell}
, it follows by the definition of Demuškin group that there is an isomorphism

H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell),\mu_{\ell^{n}})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}.

Therefore (
2.6
) implies that the injections
\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
are isomorphisms. By taking inverse limit over all
n
and the fact that
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
is the
\prod_{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)
for all primes
\ell\nmid p|\Gamma|
, we have

H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)(\ell),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\quad\text{and}\quad H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}},

and they both have trivial actions of
\Frob_{k}
and
\Gamma
.
Then by the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence, we have

H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})=H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})^{\Gamma}\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}},

\text{and similarly,}\quad H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell},

which proves (
2.3
) and (
2.4
).

Let
G
denote
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
. Since the class group of
\overline{k}(t)
is trivial,
G^{ab}
is generated by all the inertia subgroups of the maximal abelian subextension of
K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)
. Since
K^{\prime}_{\O}/K\overline{k}
is unramified, each inertia subgroup of
K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)
is isomorphic to its image in
\Gal(K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t))\simeq\Gamma
. So the exponent of
G^{ab}
divides
|\Gamma|
, and hence
\Ext_{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{1}(G^{ab},\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})=0
. Because
G
acts trivially on
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, it follows by the universal coefficient theorem and (
2.4
) that, for any
n
that is prime to
p|\Gamma|
,

\Hom(H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq H^{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})=\mu_{n}.

So
H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})/nH_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq\mu_{n}
, and by taking the inverse limit over all such
n
, we have

H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}.

By applying the same argument, we can prove
H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)
.
∎

Corollary 2.3
.

There are isomorphisms

H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})\simeq\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\quad\text{and}\quad H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}(\ell)/k(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}.

Proof.

Consider the short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\longrightarrow\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))\longrightarrow\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))\longrightarrow 1

Since
\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))
is a free group generated by
\Frob_{k}
, it has cohomological dimension 1. Then by the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence (e.g. by
[
28
, Chap.II, §4, Ex.4]
), there is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t)),H^{1}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}))

(2.7)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})\longrightarrow H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})^{\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))}\longrightarrow 0.

Recall that
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\simeq\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/K\overline{k})\rtimes\Gamma
, where
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/K\overline{k})
is pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
.
We showed in the proof of Lemma
2.2
that the abelianization of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
has exponent dividing
|\Gamma|
, so

H^{1}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})=\Hom((\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}))=0.

So, the first isomorphism in the corollary follows by (
2.7
) and Lemma
2.2
,
and the second isomorphism in the corollary follows by similar arguments.
∎

We recall the definition of admissible
\Gamma
-groups in
[
21
]
: for a finite group
\Gamma
, a profinite
\Gamma
-group is
admissible
if it is of order prime to
|\Gamma|
and is topologically generated, under the
\Gamma
action, by the elements in the form of
g^{-1}\gamma(g)
for
g\in G,\gamma\in\Gamma
. In particular, for an admissible
\Gamma
-group
H
, one can check that
(H\rtimes\Gamma)^{ab}\simeq\Gamma^{ab}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

(1)

The action of
\Gal(K/\overline{k}(t))\simeq\Gamma
on
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
makes
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
an admissible
\Gamma
-group.

(2)

The
(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
-Schur covering of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
has kernel isomorphic to
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
:

1\longrightarrow\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\longrightarrow 1.

(2.8)

Moreover, such
(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
-Schur covering is unique up to isomorphism; that is, if there are two such coverings
\pi_{i}:\widetilde{G}_{i}\to\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
for
i=1,2
, then there is an isomorphism
\phi:G_{1}\to G_{2}
such that
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}\circ\phi
.

(3)

There exists a unique
\Frob_{k}
action on the group
\widetilde{G}
in (
2.8
), with which (
2.8
) becomes a short exact sequence of
\Frob_{k}
-groups

1\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\longrightarrow 1.

(2.9)

Proof.

We let
H
denote
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
and
G
denote
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
, so
G\simeq H\rtimes\Gamma
.
The subgroup of
H
generated by elements
\{h^{-1}\gamma(h)\mid h\in H,\gamma\in\Gamma\}
is the commutator subgroup
[H,\Gamma]
of
G
, which is a normal subgroup contained in
H
. Then
H/[H,\Gamma]
is a quotient of
H
with the trivial action of
\Gamma
, and hence it is a quotient of
G
.
However, such quotient corresponds to an unramified extension of
\overline{k}(t)
, which must be trivial.
So
H=[H,\Gamma]
, which proves (
1
).

We denote
A:=\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. Note that
H^{2}(G,A)\simeq\Ext(G,A)
; and to see this correspondence explicitly, we can construct a group extension for every cohomology class
c
. For each
c\in H^{2}(G,A)
, there is a normalized 2-cocycle
\alpha
contained in
c
, i.e., a cocycle such that
\alpha(g,1)=\alpha(1,g)=0
for any
g\in G
. Then the equivalent class of group extensions corresponding to
c
can be represented by a group
\widetilde{G}
with underlying set
A\times G
and the multiplicative rule

(a,x)(b,y)=(a+x(b)+\alpha(x,y),xy),

and the extension

1\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}G\longrightarrow 1,

(2.10)

where the surjection
\pi
is the projection to the second component.
Since
G
acts trivially on
A
, (
2.10
) is a central extension, so it is a stem extension if and only if the central extension reduced from (
2.10
) obtained by modulo
mA
from
\widetilde{G}
is nonsplit for any
m\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, which happens exactly when the image of
c
in
H^{2}(G,A/mA)
is nonzero. From the proof of Lemma
2.2
, we see that, forgetting the
\Frob_{k}
-action,

H^{2}(G,A/mA)\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/m{\mathbb{Z}}

as groups for any
m\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
.
Thus, we see that (
2.10
) is a stem extension if and only if the image of
c
under the isomorphism (
2.4
) is contained in
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. We denote the set of these classes corresponding to stem extensions by
C
.

Next, we show that the group extension (
2.10
) defined by all the cohomological classes in
C
are isomorphic to each other. We pick a
c
in
C
, let
\alpha
be the normalized
2
-cocycle in
c
and let
\widetilde{G}
be the group extension defined by
\alpha
. Then for any
d\in C
, we can write
d
as
mc
for some
m\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, and
m\alpha
is the normalized 2-cocycle contained in
d
. We let
\widetilde{G}_{d}
represent the group extension defined by
m\alpha
. Then there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{G}_{d}

\displaystyle\text{defined by}\quad(a,1)

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle(ma,1)\quad\text{for all $a\in A$}

\displaystyle(0,x)

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle(0,x)\quad\text{for all $x\in G$,}

which clearly gives an isomorphism between the extensions
\widetilde{G}\twoheadrightarrow G
and
\widetilde{G}_{d}\twoheadrightarrow G
. Then by (
2.5
), these stem extensions are
(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
-Schur coverings, so we finish the proof of the statement (
2
).

Denote
A(1):=\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. We can apply the argument above about group extensions to
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))
and see that the each equivalence classes of group extensions

1\longrightarrow A(1)\longrightarrow\hat{G}\overset{\hat{\pi}}{\longrightarrow}\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))\longrightarrow 1

(2.11)

is represented by a class in
H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t)),A(1))
. By taking restriction, we get a group extension

1\longrightarrow A(1)\longrightarrow\hat{\pi}^{-1}(G)\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow 1,

(2.12)

and it is a central extension since
G
acts trivially on
A(1)
. So (
2.12
) is a stem extension if and only if the extension obtained by quotienting
\hat{G}
in (
2.11
) by
mA(1)
is nonsplit for any
m
, which happens exactly when the image of the corresponding cohomology class
c
in
H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t)),\mu_{m})
is nonzero for any
m\in\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
.
From the proof of Lemma
2.2
and Corollary
2.3
, we see that

H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t)),\mu_{m})\simeq H^{2}(G,\mu_{m})^{\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/m{\mathbb{Z}}.

So (
2.12
) is a stem extension if and only if the image of
c
under the first isomorphism in Corollary
2.3
is contained in
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
. Then by using the same argument in the proof of the statement (
2
), we see that all of the extensions (
2.11
) corresponding to such cohomological classes are isomorphic.

We fix an extension (
2.11
) such that (
2.12
) is a stem extension. Recall that we identify
\Frob_{k}
as an element of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))
whose image in
\Gal(\overline{k}(t)/k(t))
is a generator. Then we pick an element
f
in
\hat{\pi}^{-1}(\Frob_{k})
. Since
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))=G\rtimes\langle{\Frob_{k}}\rangle
and
f
generate a free subgroup of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/k(t))
, we have that

\hat{G}=\hat{\pi}^{-1}(G)\rtimes\langle{f}\rangle,

which gives an action of
f
(defined by conjugation) on every term of (
2.12
).
This
f
-action does not depend on the choice of
f
, because two different choices of
f
differ by an element of
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, which is contained in the center of
\hat{\pi}^{-1}(G)
. So this unique
f
-action on (
2.12
) gives the exact sequence in the statement (
3
).
∎

The following is a group theoretical property of stem extensions that will be useful in this paper.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
1\to A\to\widetilde{G}\to G\to 1
be a stem extension. If
H
is a subgroup of
\widetilde{G}
that maps surjectively to
G
, then
H=\widetilde{G}
.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{G}=AH
, all the conjugates of the subgroup
H
are in the form of
a^{-1}Ha
for
a\in A
. However,
A
is in the center of
\widetilde{G}
, so
a^{-1}Ha=H
, which implies that
H
is a normal subgroup of
\widetilde{G}
. If
H
is a proper subgroup of
\widetilde{G}
, then
\widetilde{G}/H
is a non-trivial abelian group, so
A
is not contained in the commutator subgroup of
\widetilde{G}
, which contradicts to the assumption that the group extension is stem.
∎

The function field extension
K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t)
corresponds to a ramified Galois
\Gamma
-cover
C\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}
, and we let
P_{1},\cdots,P_{m}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}
denote the branch loci of this covering.
So there is a natural surjection

\pi^{\et}_{1}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}-\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}\})\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t)).

By the Grothendieck’s theory of specialization and the description of the fundamental group of a Riemann surface,
the pro-prime-to-
p
completion of the étale fundamental group above, which we will denote by
\Omega_{K}
, is a free pro-prime-to-
p
group on
m-1
generators and has a presentation

\Omega_{K}:=\pi^{\et}_{1}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}-\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}\})_{(p)^{\prime}}=\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\mid g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{m}=1\rangle

(2.13)

where the image of
g_{i}
in
\Omega_{K}
is a generator of an inertia group of
P_{i}
, and is mapped to a generator of an inertia subgroup at
P_{i}
of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
(and resp. of
\Gal(K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t))
).
We call the tuple
\underline{g}:=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{m})
satisfing the description above for an arbitrary ordering of
P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}
by
a system of inertia generators for
K
. The systems of inertia generators are not unique.
The Frobenius
\Frob_{k}
acts on each Galois group, and hence we obtain a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant surjection when we choose compatible lifts of
\Frob_{k}

\varpi_{K}:\Omega_{K}\longrightarrow\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)).

(2.14)

By (
2.13
),
\Omega_{K}
is a quotient of the free pro-prime-to-
p
group generated by
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
. So we obtain a surjection of groups

\rho_{K}:\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\rangle\longrightarrow\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),

(2.15)

which factors through
\varpi_{K}
. The map
\rho_{K}
is just a group surjection, because the free group
\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\rangle
does not have any natural
\Frob_{k}
-action.

Let
z=t-a_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in\overline{k}
corresponds to the branch locus
P_{i}
. Then an inertia subgroup
\Gal\left(k((z^{1/\infty}))/k((z))\right)
at
P_{i}
of
\pi^{\et}_{1}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\overline{k}}-\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}\})_{(p)^{\prime}}
is naturally identified with
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p)^{\prime}}
via

\displaystyle\Gal\left(k((z^{1/\infty}))/k((z))\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p)^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m}\frac{\sigma(\sqrt[m]{z})}{\sqrt[m]{z}}.

Under these identifications, by
[
37
, §5.1 & 5.2]
, in the presentation (
2.13
), each inertia generator
g_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,m
maps to a common generator of
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p)^{\prime}}
. For a system of inertia generators
\underline{g}=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{m})
, we let
I(\underline{g})
denote the common generator of
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p)^{\prime}}
.

For a central group extension
\pi:G\to H
with an isomorphism
\ker\pi\simeq A
for some abelian group
A
, the
differential map
d_{\pi}
of
\pi
is the homomorphism

d_{\pi}:H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}})\to A

that is the image of the class containing
\pi
under the quotient map
H^{2}(G,A)\to\Hom(H_{2}(G,{\mathbb{Z}}),A)
in the universal coefficient theorem.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
\widetilde{G}
,
\pi
be as described in the group extension (
2.8
) in Lemma
2.4
, and let
\rho_{K}
be the surjection (
2.15
).

(1)

Then there is a unique surjection

\widetilde{\rho}_{K}:\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\rangle\longrightarrow\widetilde{G},

such that
\rho_{K}=\pi\circ\widetilde{\rho}_{K}
and
\ord(\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{i}))=\ord(\rho_{K}(g_{i}))
for each
i=1,\ldots,m
.

(2)

The kernel of
\pi
is generated by
\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})
. So we can define a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism

\displaystyle\iota_{\pi}:\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\ker\pi

(2.16)

\displaystyle I(\underline{g})

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m}).

Moreover, this isomorphism is independent of the choice of the system of inertia generators.

(3)

The differential map
d_{\pi}:H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})\to\ker\pi
of
\pi
factors through a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism
H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to\ker\pi
, which by abuse of notation will be also denoted by
d_{\pi}
. Then the
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism
\omega_{K}:=d_{\pi}^{-1}\circ\iota_{\pi}:\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
does not depend on the choice of the stem extension
\pi
.

Remark 2.7
.

The isomorphism
\iota_{\pi}
identifies
\widetilde{G}
as a group extension of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
with kernel
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, and hence corresponds to an element
\epsilon
in
H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})
.
Then the differential map
H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
obtained from
\epsilon
is the inverse of
\omega_{K}
.

Proof.

We let
\overline{g}_{i}
be the image of
g_{i}
in
\Omega_{K}
.
We denote
t_{i}:=\varpi_{K}(\overline{g}_{i})
for each
i
(
\varpi_{K}
is the map (
2.14
)), which is a generator of an inertia subgroup at
P_{i}
of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
. Then
\ord(t_{i})
equals the order of its image in
\Gal(K\overline{k}/\overline{k}(t))\simeq\Gamma
because
K^{\prime}_{\O}
is unramified over
K\overline{k}
. By definition
\rho_{K}
is a lift of
\varpi_{K}
,
\rho_{K}(g_{i})=t_{i}
and hence
\ord(\rho_{K}(g_{i}))=\ord(t_{i})
.
Consider the central group extension (
2.8
). The kernel has order prime to
|\Gamma|
, so for each
i
, there exists a unique lift
\widetilde{t}_{i}\in\pi^{-1}(t_{i})
of
t_{i}
in
\widetilde{G}
such that
\ord(\widetilde{t}_{i})=\ord(t_{i})
. The elements
\widetilde{t}_{i}
’s generate a subgroup of
\widetilde{G}
that maps surjectively to
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
, and therefore by Lemma
2.5
, they generate
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
because (
2.8
) is a stem extension.
So the homomorphism
\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\rangle\to\widetilde{G}
mapping
g_{i}
to
\widetilde{t}_{i}
is a surjection, and is the unique surjection satisfies the conditions for
\widetilde{\rho}_{K}
given in (
1
).

Note that
\rho_{K}
factors through
\widetilde{\rho}_{K}
, so
r:=\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})
in contained in the subgroup
\ker\pi\simeq\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
of
\widetilde{G}
. Define
\widehat{G}
to be the quotient of
\widetilde{G}
modulo
r
. Then
\widehat{G}
fits into the following commutative diagram, where each arrow represents a surjection.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/g_{1}\cdots g_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\rho}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/r}

(2.17)

By the properties of
\widetilde{\rho}_{K}
, the image of
g_{i}
in
\widehat{G}
has order equal to
\ord(t_{i})
. So the surjection
s
defines a
\widehat{G}
-extension
L/\overline{k}(t)
that contains
K^{\prime}_{\O}
as a subfield, and its inertia subgroup at
P_{i}
maps isomorphically to the inertia subgroup at
P_{i}
of
K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)
. Thus,
L
is unramified over
K^{\prime}_{\O}
and hence is unramified over
K\overline{k}
, and moreover,
\Gal(L/K\overline{k})
is pro-prime-to-
(p|\Gamma|)
. By definition of
K^{\prime}_{\O}
, we see that
L
must be
K^{\prime}_{\O}
, so
r
generates
\ker\pi
.

Assume that
\underline{h}:=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{m})
is another system of inertia generators. Then there exists
\alpha\in\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)^{\times}_{(p)^{\prime}}
such that
I(\underline{h})=I(\underline{g})^{\alpha}
, and there is a permutation
\sigma\in S_{m}
such that
h_{i}
is conjugate to
g_{\sigma(i)}^{\alpha}
for all
i
.
Then
\underline{h}
defines a surjection
\rho^{\prime}_{K}:\langle h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\rangle\to\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
, where
h_{i}
maps to the corresponding inertia generator, and
\rho^{\prime}_{K}
lifts to a surjection
\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}:\langle h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\rangle\to\widetilde{G}
such that
\ord(\rho^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i}))=\ord(\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i}))
for each
i
. To prove the last claim in the statement (
2
), we need to show
\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{1}\cdots h_{m})=\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})^{\alpha}
.

Recall that
\Omega_{K}
is a free pro-prime-to-
p
group on
m-1
generators. By Lemma
2.5
, the surjection
\varpi_{K}
in (
2.14
) lifts to a surjection
\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}:\Omega_{K}\to\widetilde{G}
such that
\varpi_{K}=\pi\circ\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}
. For each
i
, we let
\overline{h}_{i}
denote the image of
h_{i}
in
\Omega_{K}
and then define

z_{i}:=\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{h}_{i})^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i}).

Because both of
\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{h}_{i})
and
\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i})
have image
\rho^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i})
in
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
,
z_{i}
is contained in
\ker\pi
, so is in the center of
\widetilde{G}
. Thus,

\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{1}\cdots h_{m})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{h}_{i})z_{i}=\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{h}_{1}\cdots\overline{h}_{m})z_{1}\cdots z_{m}=z_{1}\cdots z_{m}.

Similarly, we define
w_{i}:=\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{g}_{i})^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{i})
for each
i
. Then
w_{i}
is in the center of
\widetilde{G}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})=w_{1}\cdots w_{m}
. Now, we fix an index
i
and suppose
j=\sigma(i)
.
There exists
x\in\Omega_{K}
such that
\overline{h}_{i}=x^{-1}\overline{g}_{j}^{\alpha}x
.
We pick the lift
y=\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(x)
of
\varpi_{K}(x)
. Then
y^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{\alpha}y
has order equal to
\ord(\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j}))=\ord(\rho_{K}(g_{j}))=\ord(\rho^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i}))
and is a lift of
\rho^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i})
, so
y^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{\alpha}y=\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i})
. Then

\displaystyle z_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{h}_{i})^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}^{\prime}_{K}(h_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{-1}\widetilde{\varpi}_{K}(\overline{g}_{j})^{-\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{\alpha}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{-1}w_{j}\left(\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{-1}w_{j}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})\right)\left(\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{-2}w_{j}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{2}\right)\cdots\left(\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{1-\alpha}w_{j}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{j})^{\alpha-1}\right)y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{j}^{\alpha}.

So we finish the proof of (
2
).

Since
\pi
defines a stem extension, its corresponding differential map is surjective, so
d_{\pi}
defined in (
3
) is a
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism because of (
2.5
) in Lemma
2.2
.
Suppose there are two stem extensions
1\to A_{i}\to\widetilde{G}_{i}\overset{\pi_{i}}{\to}\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))\to 1
for
i=1,2
. By Lemma
2.4
(
2
) and (
3
), there exists an
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism
\phi:\widetilde{G}_{1}\to\widetilde{G}_{2}
such that
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}\circ\phi
, so
\phi|_{A_{1}}:A_{1}\to A_{2}
is an isomorphism. Then the differential map
d_{\pi_{i}}
satisfies
d_{\pi_{2}}=\phi|_{A_{1}}\circ d_{\pi_{1}}
.
For each
i
, let
\iota_{\pi_{i}}
denote the map in (
2.16
) for
\pi_{i}
. Then by definition of
\iota_{\pi_{i}}
, we obtain that
\iota_{\pi_{2}}=\phi|_{A_{1}}\circ\iota_{\pi_{1}}
. Thus
d^{-1}_{\pi_{1}}\circ\iota_{\pi_{1}}=d^{-1}_{\pi_{2}}\circ\iota_{\pi_{2}}
and then we proved (
3
).
∎

Since the map
\omega_{K}
in Proposition
2.6
does not depend on the choice of
\pi
and the system of inertia generators, it is an invariant associated to
K
.

Definition 2.8

(Invariant
\omega_{K}
associated to
K
)
.

We define the
invariant
\omega_{K}
associated to
K
to be the
\Frob_{k}
-equivariant isomorphism
\omega_{K}:\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
in Proposition
2.6
(
3
).

2.2.
Cohomological definition of
\omega_{K}

As discussed in Remark
2.7
, the inverse of
\omega_{K}
is the differential map of a class
\epsilon\in H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})
. In this subsection, we identify
\epsilon
as a class in the étale cohomology
H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{k}},\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}})
for the curve
X_{\overline{k}}
whose function field is
K\overline{k}
, and we compute the trace of this class in Corollary
2.12
.

Lemma 2.9
.

Use the notation in Proposition
2.6
.
Let
\overline{g}_{i}\in\Omega_{K}
denote the image of
g_{i}
under the quotient map defined by the presentation (
2.13
).
Then there is a surjection
\varrho_{K}
that factors through
\rho_{K}
and is defined by

\displaystyle\varrho_{K}:\Omega_{K}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{i}

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{i})\quad\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,m-1.

In particular, we have the following identity

\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})=\varrho_{K}(\overline{g}_{m})^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{m}).

Proof.

We consider the injective homomorphism

\displaystyle\varepsilon:\Omega_{K}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\langle{g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}}\rangle

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{i}

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle g_{i}\quad\text{ for }i=1,\ldots,m-1.

Then the composition map
\varrho_{K}:=\widetilde{\rho}_{K}\circ\varepsilon
is a homomorphism from
\Omega_{K}
to
\widetilde{G}
such that
\varrho_{K}(\overline{g}_{i})=\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{i})
for
i=1,\ldots,m-1
. Then, since
\rho_{K}(g_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,m-1
generate
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
, it follows by Lemma
2.5
that
\varrho_{K}
is a surjection.
Because
\overline{g}_{1}\cdots\overline{g}_{m}=1
, we see that
\varrho_{K}(\overline{g}_{m})=\varrho_{K}(\overline{g}_{1}\cdots\overline{g}_{m-1})^{-1}=\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})^{-1}\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{m})
.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
\widetilde{G}
and
\pi
be as in the group extension (
2.8
) in Lemma
2.4
. We define
\widetilde{H}:=\pi^{-1}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K))
and
\varpi:=\pi|_{\widetilde{H}}
, and obtain the following stem extension of
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)

1\longrightarrow\ker\varpi\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}\overset{\varpi}{\longrightarrow}G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)\longrightarrow 1.

(2.18)

Then, for any integer
n
satisfying
\gcd(n,p|\Gamma|)=1
, the transgression map defined by the extension
\varpi

tg_{\varpi,n}:H^{1}(\ker\varpi,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\longrightarrow H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We first show that (
2.18
) is a stem extension. It is central because (
2.8
) is central. So it suffices to prove that
\ker\varpi
is contained in
[\widetilde{H},\widetilde{H}]
, or equivalently, that the homomorphism of abelianizations
\widetilde{H}^{ab}\to G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}
induced by
\varpi
is an isomorphism. Let
N
denote the kernel of
\widetilde{H}^{ab}\to G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}
. Then the short exact sequence
1\to N\to\widetilde{H}^{ab}\to G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}\to 1
has an action of
\Gamma
inherited from
\varpi
, and in particular, the
\Gamma
action on
N
is trivial. Because
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
is an admissible
\Gamma
-group (Lemma
2.4
(
1
)), its abelianization is a
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[\Gamma]
-module whose
\Gamma
-invariant is trivial. Then, since
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[\Gamma]
is a semisimple ring, we have

\widetilde{H}^{ab}\simeq N\oplus G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}.

(2.19)

On the other hand, by taking quotient of
\widetilde{G}
in (
2.8
) modulo
[\widetilde{H},\widetilde{H}]
, we obtain a short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{ab}\rtimes\Gamma\longrightarrow G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}\rtimes\Gamma\longrightarrow 1,

which is a stem extension because
\pi
is stem. The isomorphism (
2.19
) implies that the abelianization of
\widetilde{H}^{ab}\rtimes\Gamma
is isomorphic to
N\times\Gamma^{ab}
. Therefore,
N
must be trivial, so (
2.18
) is stem.

Consider the transgression map
tg_{\pi,n}:H^{1}(\ker\pi,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})
defined by
\pi
. Since
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))^{ab}\simeq\Gamma^{ab}
and we’ve shown that
\pi
is the pro-prime-to-
(p|\Gamma|)
Schur covering of
\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))
, the prime-to-
(p|\Gamma|)
part of the Schur multiplier of
\widetilde{G}
is trivial
[
16
, Chap.10, Prop.1.12]
. So
H^{2}(\widetilde{G},{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})=0
, and then
tg_{\pi,n}
is surjective.
On the other hand, since
\pi
is a stem extension,
\widetilde{G}^{ab}\simeq\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t))^{ab}
, so the inflation map
H^{1}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{1}(\widetilde{G},{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})
is an isomorphism, which shows that
tg_{\pi,n}
is an isomorphism.

Finally, consider the exact sequence

0\to H^{1}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{1}(\widetilde{H},{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{1}(\ker\varpi,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\overset{tg_{\varpi,n}}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\to\cdots.

Because
\widetilde{H}^{ab}\simeq G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)^{ab}
,
tg_{\varpi,n}
is injective.
Also,

tg_{\pi,n}
is the composition map

H^{1}(\ker\pi,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\ker\varpi,{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\overset{tg_{\varpi,n}}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}})\overset{\res}{\longrightarrow}H^{2}(\Gal(K^{\prime}_{\O}/\overline{k}(t)),{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}).

Recall that we’ve shown in the proof of Lemma
2.2
that the map
\res
is an isomorphism. So we see that
tg_{\pi,n}
being isomorphic and
tg_{\varpi,n}
being injective implies that
tg_{\varpi,n}
is also an isomorphism.
∎

Use the notation in Lemmas
2.9
and
2.10
. Let
X
be the smooth projective curve over
k
whose function field is
K
, and let
X_{\overline{k}}
denote the base change of
X
to
\overline{k}
.
Assume that
n
is a positive integer relatively prime to
p|\Gamma|
.
We consider the following composition map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\ker\varpi,\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{k}},\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces tg_{\varpi,n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tr_{X,n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(2.20)

Here
tg_{\varpi,n}
is obtained by tensoring
\mu_{n}
to the transgression map in Lemma
2.10
. The middle arrow follows by the canonical isomorphism between Galois cohomology and étale cohomology and the fact (we proved in Lemma
2.2
) that the inflation map
H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{n})\to H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{n})
is an isomorphism.
The last arrow is the trace obtained by the Poincaré duality.

Let
{\mathfrak{i}}
denote the homomorphism

\displaystyle\ker\varpi

\displaystyle\overset{{\mathfrak{i}}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}

(2.21)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{K}(g_{1}\cdots g_{m})

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle I(\underline{g}),

and then let
{\mathfrak{i}}_{n}:\ker\varpi\to\mu_{n}
denote the composition of
{\mathfrak{i}}
and the natural surjection
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to\mu_{n}
.

Lemma 2.11
.

The image of
{\mathfrak{i}}_{n}
under (
2.20
) is
-|\Gamma|
mod
n
.

Proof.

We first recall the definition of
\tr_{X,n}
. Let
C^{1}(X_{\overline{k}})
be the free group on the set of prime 1-cycles on
X_{\overline{k}}
. Then the cycle map
\cl_{X}:C^{1}(X_{\overline{k}})\to H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{k}},\mu_{n})
is the composition of the canonical maps
C^{1}(X_{\overline{k}})\to\Pic(X_{\overline{k}})
and
\Pic(X_{\overline{k}})\to H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{k}},\mu_{n})
, and
\cl_{X}
maps a prime 1-cycle to the element whose
\tr_{X,n}
-trace is 1. Rewriting everything in Galois cohomology, these two maps are two connecting homomorphisms.
We let
S
denote the set of all places of
K\overline{k}
, and for any extension
L
of
K\overline{k}
, we let
S(L)
denote the set of all places of
L
.
Then the first map
C^{1}(X_{\overline{k}})\to\Pic(X_{\overline{k}})
is the connecting homomorphism

\delta_{1}:\quad\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S}{\mathbb{Z}}=H^{0}\left(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{P}}\in S(K_{\O})}{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\longrightarrow H^{1}\left(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}\right)

associated to the short exact sequence of
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\O}^{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{P}}\in S(K_{\O})}{\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus\ord_{{\mathfrak{P}}}}

(2.22)

The second map
\Pic(X_{\overline{k}})\to H^{2}_{\et}(X_{\overline{k}},\mu_{n})
is

\delta_{2}:\quad H^{1}\left(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}\right)\longrightarrow H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{n}),

associated to the Kummer sequence
1\to\mu_{n}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}\overset{n}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}\to 1
.

There is a short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow G^{\prime}_{S}(K)\longrightarrow G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)\longrightarrow 1,

where
G^{\prime}_{S}(K)
denotes
G_{S}(K\overline{k})_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
.
Applying the Hochschild–Serre exact sequence, we obtain

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow H^{1}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{1}(G^{\prime}_{S}(K),\mu_{n})\longrightarrow H^{1}(N,\mu_{n})^{G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)}

(2.23)

\displaystyle\overset{tg}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{n})\longrightarrow 0,

where the last arrow uses the fact that
G^{\prime}_{S}(K)
is a free pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
group.
By the Riemann existence theorem (a prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
analogue of
[
28
, Thm.(10.1.10)]
),

N
is the free product of the prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completions of inertia subgroups at places in
S(K^{\prime}_{\O})
, so

H^{1}(N,\mu_{n})^{G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)}=\left(\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{P}}\in S(K^{\prime}_{\O})}H^{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}},\mu_{n})\right)^{G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)}=\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S}H^{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mu_{n}),

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}
) is the pro-prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of the local inertia group at
{\mathfrak{p}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{P}}
).
The last equality above is because
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}}={\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
for each
{\mathfrak{P}}|{\mathfrak{p}}
and
G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)
acts transitively and freely on all the primes above
{\mathfrak{p}}
, which both follow by the fact that
K^{\prime}_{\O}/K\overline{k}
is split completely at all primes.
For a place
{\mathfrak{p}}\in S
, we let
(K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
denote the completion of
K\overline{k}
at
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Let
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
be a uniformizer of the prime
{\mathfrak{p}}
of
(K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Then
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the prime-to-
p|\Gamma|
completion of the
\Gal\left((K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}[a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1/\infty}]/(K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\right)
, so there is a canonical isomorphism
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{(p|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
arising from the action of
\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)
as ring automorphisms of
(K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}[a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1/\infty}]
over
(K\overline{k})_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
So we can define a surjection

\displaystyle f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mu_{n})

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\left(\sigma\mapsto\frac{\sigma(\sqrt[n]{a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}})}{\sqrt[n]{a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}\right)

We will prove by explicitly computing the cocycles that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S}{\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}\left(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}^{\times}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S}H^{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{n}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces tg}

(2.24)

where the lower map
tg
is the composition of
tg
in (
2.23
) and the canonical isomorphism
H^{2}(G^{\prime}_{\O}(K),\mu_{n})\simeq H^{2}(G_{\O}(K\overline{k}),\mu_{n})
.

Let
1_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
denote the element
1
in the component
{\mathbb{Z}}
for
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then
f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})\in H^{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mu_{n})\subset H^{1}(N,\mu_{n})^{G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)}=\Hom_{G^{\prime}_{S}(K)}(N,\mu_{n})
is a surjection from
N\to\mu_{n}
whose kernel is closed under the conjugation in
G^{\prime}_{S}(K)
. Let
L
denote the field fixed by
\ker f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
. By the Kummer theory, there exists
b\in K^{\prime}_{\O}
such that
L=K^{\prime}_{\O}(\sqrt[n]{b})
and
\ord_{{\mathfrak{P}}}(b)=1
for any
{\mathfrak{P}}\in S(K^{\prime}_{\O})
above
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then the isomorphism
\Gal(L/K^{\prime}_{\O})\simeq\mu_{n}
induced by
f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
is defined by
g\mapsto\frac{g(\sqrt[n]{b})}{\sqrt[n]{b}}
for
g\in\Gal(L/K^{\prime}_{\O})
.
So we have a central group extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gal(L/K\overline{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

and we pick a splitting
t:G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)\to\Gal(L/K\overline{k})
. Then
tg\circ f_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
is the class corresponding to this central extension, and explicitly, the class containing the 2-cocyle

\displaystyle z:G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)\times G^{\prime}_{\O}(K)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mu_{n}

\displaystyle(\sigma,\tau)

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\frac{t(\sigma\tau)^{-1}t(\sigma)t(\tau)(\sqrt[n]{b})}{\sqrt[n]{b}}.

On the other hand, let’s compute
\delta_{2}\circ\delta_{1}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
. The image of
b
under the quotient map in (
2.22
) is invariant under the
G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
action. Then, because we assume
\ord_{{\mathfrak{P}}}(b)=1
, the image of
b
is
1_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
in
\oplus_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in S}{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then by chasing the diagram for the connecting homomorphism
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{1}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
is the class containing the 1-cocycle

\displaystyle x:G_{\O}(K\overline{k})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}^{\times}}

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma(b)}{b}.

We choose a splitting
s:{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{K_{\O}}^{\times}
of the Kummer sequence such that
s\left(\frac{\sigma(b)}{b}\right)=\frac{t(\sigma)(\sqrt[n]{b})}{\sqrt[n]{b}}
for any
\sigma\in G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
. There exists such a splitting because
\left(\frac{t(\sigma)(\sqrt[n]{b})}{\sqrt[n]{b}}\right)^{n}=\frac{\sigma(b)}{b}
. Then by chasing the diagram for
\delta_{2}
, we see that
\delta_{2}\circ\delta_{1}(1_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
