\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma)
, it obtains a measure from the measure on
\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};1)
, which is the “Haar” measure in the definition.

(2)

Since
\ell
is odd,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
is a characteristic subgroup of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
and then
\phi
preserves
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
. Any action on
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
is trivial, so
\ker(1-\phi)
is contained in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
for any
\phi
.

5.2.
Computing the moment of the random group

Lemma 5.5
.

Consider the Schur covering
1\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\overset{\pi_{n}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\to 1
. Let
\xi_{n}
be a generator of
\ker\pi_{n}
. Let
H
be a pro-
\ell
group,
\varpi:\widetilde{H}\to H
a Schur covering of
H
, and
\rho:{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\to H
a group surjection.

(1)

Then
\rho
can be lifted to a surjection
\widetilde{\rho}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\to\widetilde{H}
such that
\varpi\circ\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\circ\pi_{n}
. Moreover, the image
\widetilde{\rho}(\xi_{n})
in
\widetilde{H}
does not depend on the choice of the lift
\widetilde{\rho}
.

(2)

Given
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)
, let
\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}\in\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},\widetilde{H})
be their lifts respectively. Assume that
\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(\xi_{n})=\widetilde{\rho}_{2}(\xi_{n})
. Then for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\times}
with
\ord(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(\xi_{n}))\mid q-1
, there exists
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
such that

(a)

\widetilde{\varphi}
preserves
\ker\pi_{n}
and induces
\varphi\in\Aut({\mathcal{G}}_{n})
such that
\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}\circ\varphi
, and

(b)

\widetilde{\varphi}(\xi_{n})=\xi_{n}^{q}
.

We will give the proof of this lemma in §
5.3
, which heavily uses the method in
[
18
]
of successive approximation on the filtration of lower
\ell
-central series of pro-
\ell
groups.

Let
H
be a finite
\ell
-group with an action of
\Gamma
, and let
\pi\in\Sur(X_{q,n},H\rtimes\Gamma)
(or
\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(Y_{q,n},H)
when we fix the action of
\Gamma
on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
). Consider the composition map
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma\overset{1-\phi}{\longrightarrow}X_{q,n}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}H\rtimes\Gamma
and let
\varpi:\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma\to H\rtimes\Gamma
be an
\ell
-Schur covering. By Lemma
2.14
, there is a surjection
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma\to\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma
satisfing the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker\varpi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\rtimes\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\circ(1-\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi}

such that the first vertical arrow in the diagram is the composition of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker\varpi,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\pi_{n}}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coinf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\varpi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the middle map
\coinf
is induced by the quotient map
\pi\circ(1-\phi)
. We denote the composition of the first two maps by
\pi_{\dagger}:{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)
. Because the sequence is
\phi
-equivariant,
\pi_{\dagger}
factors through
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}/(q-1){\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
H
be a finite
\ell
-group with an action of
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
that acts on
H^{ab}
as inversion.
Let
n
be a sufficiently large integer such that there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant surjection from
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
to
H
.
Let
\delta
be a group homomorphism
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)[q-1]
.
Then

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur(X_{q,n},H\rtimes\Gamma)\mid\pi_{\dagger}=\delta\right\}\right)=1

Also, if we fix a homomorphic splitting of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma\to\Gamma
(that is, we fix the action of
\Gamma
on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
), then

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(Y_{q,n},H)\mid\pi_{\dagger}=\delta\right\}\right)=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]}.

Proof.

For any
\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)
, by Lemma
2.14
, there exists
\widetilde{\pi}\in\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma)
such that
\pi\circ\pi_{n}=\varpi\circ\widetilde{\pi}
.
Let’s prove that there is a unique such
\widetilde{\pi}
.
Let
S
be the closed subgroup of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
generated by all elements of order
2
. Then
S
is normal because the set of all the order-
2
elements are closed under conjugation. The quotient
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma/S
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant pro-
\ell
quotient of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
with trivial induced
\Gamma
action. However,
\Gamma
acts on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}^{ab}
as inversion and any non-trivial pro-
\ell
group has non-trivial abelianization, so
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
does not have any non-trivial quotient on which the induced
\Gamma
-action is trivial. So we have
S=\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
, which implies that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
is generated by order-2 elements.
Also, for each order-2 element
x
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
, we have that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
is the product of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
and
\langle x\rangle
, so
\pi\circ\pi_{n}(x)
is non-trivial and hence has order 2.
Each order-2 element of
H\rtimes\Gamma
lifts to a unique order-2 element of
\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma
because
\widetilde{H}\to H
is an
\ell
-Schur covering. Therefore,
\widetilde{\pi}
must map each order-2 element
x
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
to the unique lift of
\pi\circ\pi_{n}(x)
, which finishes the proof of the uniqueness of
\widetilde{\pi}
. So for each
\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)
, we let
\widetilde{\pi}
denote its unique lift
\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma)
.

Next, we prove the first equality in the theorem. Let
h=d_{\varpi}\circ\delta(\xi_{n})
.
Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur(X_{q,n},H\rtimes\Gamma)\mid\pi_{\dagger}=\delta\right\}\right)

(5.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)\\
\text{s.t. }\widetilde{\pi}(\xi_{n})=h\end{subarray}}\Prob(\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)\mid\ker(1-\phi)\subseteq\ker\widetilde{\pi}).

The condition
\ker(1-\phi)\subseteq\ker\widetilde{\pi}
is equivalent to
\widetilde{\pi}\circ\phi=\widetilde{\pi}
.
We let
\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
denote the set of
\pi
as described under the sum in (
5.4
), and let
\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
. Each
\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)
passes
\widetilde{\pi}
to another surjection
\widetilde{\pi}\circ\phi\in\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\widetilde{H}\rtimes\Gamma)
, and the assumption
\ord(h)\mid q-1
implies that
\widetilde{\pi}\circ\phi(\xi_{n})=h
, so
\widetilde{\pi}\circ\phi
induces a surjection in
\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
. In other words,
\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)
acts on the set
\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
, and in particular, the stabilizer of
\pi
by this action is exactly the set of
\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)
such that
\ker(1-\phi)\subseteq\ker\widetilde{\pi}
. Thus, the probability in (
5.4
) is the inverse of the size of the orbit containing
\pi
. In the rest of the proof, we will prove that the set
\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
contains only one orbit. After proving that, it immediately follows that (
5.4
) equals 1, and hence we finish the proof of the theorem.

Let
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma;\xi_{n}\mapsto h)
, and let
\widetilde{\pi}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{2}
denote the lifts of
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
respectively.
Let
\rho_{i}
(resp.
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}
) be the restriction of
\pi_{i}
(resp.
\widetilde{\pi}_{i}
) onto
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
(resp.
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
) for
i=1,2
. Then
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}(\xi_{n})=\widetilde{\pi}_{i}(\xi_{n})=h
, so by Lemma
5.5
(
2
), there exists
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
satisfying (
2a
) and (
2b
). This automorphism
\widetilde{\varphi}
is not necessarily
\Gamma
-equivariant, however, we will show below that we can modify
\widetilde{\varphi}
to make it
\Gamma
-equivariant. Let
\varphi\in\Aut({\mathcal{G}}_{n})
be as described in Lemma
5.5
(
2a
). The automorphism
\varphi
passes the action of
\gamma
(the non-trivial element of
\Gamma
) on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
to an action of
{}^{\varphi}\gamma
which is defined by

{}^{\varphi}\gamma(g)=\varphi\circ\gamma\circ\varphi^{-1}(g)\quad\text{ for $g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{n}$}.

Because of
\rho_{2}\circ\varphi=\rho_{1}
and that
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
extend to
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
, it follows that
\gamma
and
{}^{\varphi}\gamma
both preserve
\ker\rho_{2}
and they induce the same action on
H
via the quotient map
\rho_{2}
, where the latter is because for any
g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{n}

\rho_{2}\circ\varphi\circ\gamma\circ\varphi^{-1}(g)=\rho_{1}\circ\gamma\circ\varphi^{-1}(g)=\gamma\circ\rho_{1}\circ\varphi^{-1}(x)=\gamma\circ\rho_{2}(x)=\rho_{2}\circ\gamma(x).

Also, the actions of
\gamma
and
{}^{\varphi}\gamma
induce the same action on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}^{ab}
(which is taking inversion). So, we let
M:=[{\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathcal{G}}_{n}]\cap\ker\rho_{2}
, and we see that the actions of
\gamma
and
{}^{\varphi}\gamma
induce the same action on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}/M
. By Lemma
5.1
, there exists
\tau\in\Aut({\mathcal{G}}_{n})
such that
{}^{\varphi}\gamma=\tau^{-1}\circ\gamma\circ\tau
and
\tau
preserves
M
and induces the trivial action on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}/M
. Thus,
\tau\circ\varphi
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant automorphism of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
such that
\rho_{2}\circ\tau\circ\varphi=\rho_{1}
. Because
\pi_{n}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is a Schur covering, by Lemma
5.5
(
1
), there exists
\widetilde{\tau}\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
such that
\pi_{n}\circ\widetilde{\tau}=\tau\circ\pi_{n}
. Next, we consider the actions on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
, and similarly to above arguments,
\widetilde{\varphi}
passes the action of
\gamma
on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
to an action of
{}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\gamma
. Then because
{}^{\varphi}\gamma=\tau^{-1}\circ\gamma\circ\tau
as actions on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, we have that
{}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\gamma
and
\widetilde{\tau}^{-1}\circ\gamma\circ\widetilde{\tau}
are two order-2 automorphisms of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that preserve
\ker\pi_{n}
and induce the same automorphism on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. So, by Lemma
5.1
, there exists
\sigma\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
such that
{}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\gamma=\sigma^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\tau}^{-1}\circ\gamma\circ\widetilde{\tau}\circ\sigma
and
\sigma
preserves
\ker\pi_{n}
and induces the trivial action on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. Therefore, we obtain an automorphism
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\tau}\circ\sigma\circ\widetilde{\varphi}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that respects the
\Gamma
action on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
, so
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
extends to an automorphism of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma
sending
\gamma\mapsto\gamma
. It’s only left to show that
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)
. Indeed,
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
perserves
\ker\pi_{n}
because all of
\widetilde{\tau},\sigma
and
\widetilde{\varphi}
preserve
\ker\pi_{n}
; and
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
mapping
\xi_{n}
to
\xi_{n}^{q}
follows by applying the following claim to
\kappa:=\widetilde{\tau}\circ\sigma
and by the fact that
\widetilde{\varphi}(\xi_{n})=\xi_{n}^{q}
.

Claim: Suppose that
\kappa
is an automorphism of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that perserves
\ker\pi_{n}
and induces the trivial automorphism on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}^{ab}
. Then
\kappa(\xi_{n})=\xi_{n}
.

We use the usual presentation
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n}\mid[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1},x_{2n}]\rangle
, and consider the short exact sequence defined by this presentation

1\longrightarrow R\longrightarrow F_{2n}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1,

where
F_{2n}
is the free pro-
\ell
group on
x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n}
and
R
is the closed normal subgroup generated by
\lambda:=[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1},x_{2n}]
. Then it gives the map
\pi_{n}
via

1\longrightarrow R/[R,F_{2n}]\longrightarrow F_{2n}/[R,F_{2n}]\simeq\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\overset{\pi_{n}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1.

(5.5)

The automorphism
\kappa
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
lifts to an automorphism
\widehat{\kappa}
of
F_{2n}
. Since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}^{ab}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}^{ab}=F_{2n}^{ab}
,
\widehat{\kappa}
induces the trivial automorphism on
F_{2n}^{ab}
. So the element
y_{i}:=x_{i}^{-1}\widehat{\kappa}(x_{i})
is contained in
F_{2n}^{(2)}
for each
i
. Then
\widehat{\kappa}(\lambda)=[x_{1}y_{1},x_{2}y_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1}y_{2n-1},x_{2n}y_{2n}]\equiv[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1},x_{2n}]\mod F_{2n}^{(3)}
. As
R\subset F_{2n}^{(2)}
, we have that
[R,F_{2n}]\subset F_{2n}^{(3)}
; and then the quotient map
R/[R,F_{2n}]\to R/(R\cap F_{2n}^{(3)})
is an isomorphism since both of them are isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. So
\widehat{\kappa}(\lambda)\equiv\lambda\mod F_{2n}^{(3)}
implies
\kappa
acts trivially on
\ker\pi_{n}
, which proves the claim and hence proves the first equality in the theorem.

To prove the second equality, we consider the following expression of the moment that is similar to (
5.4
)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(Y_{q,n},H)\mid\pi_{\dagger}=\delta\right\}\right)

(5.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)\\
\text{s.t. }\widetilde{\pi}(\xi_{n})=h\\
\text{and }\pi|_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}\text{ is $\Gamma$-equivariant}\end{subarray}}\Prob(\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,\pi_{n};q)\mid\ker(1-\phi)\subseteq\ker\widetilde{\pi}).

By the Schur–Zassenhaus theorem, any two homomorphic splittings of
H\times\Gamma\to\Gamma
are conjugate by an element of
H
, and therefore, the conjugation gives an automorphism of
H\rtimes\Gamma
that maps one splitting to the other. Also, because the conjugations of
H\rtimes\Gamma
acts transitively on the set of all splittings, the number of splittings is exactly
[H:H^{\Gamma}]
. So we have

\displaystyle\#\{\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)\mid\widetilde{\pi}(\xi_{n})=h\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[H:H^{\Gamma}]\#\{\pi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n}\rtimes\Gamma,H\rtimes\Gamma)\mid\widetilde{\pi}(\xi_{n})=h\text{ and $\pi|_{{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}$ is $\Gamma$-equivariant}\}.

Thus, the second equality in the theorem follows by the first equality, (
5.4
) and (
5.6
).
∎

5.3.
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Let
F_{2n}
denote the free pro-
\ell
group on
2n
generators
x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n}
. Then there is a presentation of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}

1\longrightarrow R\longrightarrow F_{2n}\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1,

(5.7)

and it induces a Schur covering of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}

1\longrightarrow R/[R,F_{2n}]\longrightarrow F_{2n}/[R,F_{2n}]\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1.

(5.8)

It follows by
[
16
, Chap.11, Thm.2.3(iii) and (iv)(b)]
that
F_{2n}/[R,F_{2n}]
can be identified with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
.
We denote the composition of the quotient map
\pi
in (
5.7
) and
\rho
by
\kappa:F_{2n}\to H
, and denote
N:=\ker\kappa
. For each
i=1,\ldots,2n
, we denote
h_{i}:=\kappa(x_{i})
and we pick an element
\widetilde{h}_{i}\in\varpi^{-1}(h_{i})
. Then the map
\widetilde{\kappa}:F_{2n}\to\widetilde{H}
defined by
x_{i}\mapsto\widetilde{h}_{i}
is a homomorphism since
F_{2n}
is free, and moreover it is surjective by Lemma
2.5
. By
[
16
, Chap.11, Thm.2.3(iv)(b)]
,
\ker\widetilde{\kappa}
contains
[N,F_{2n}]
, so
\widetilde{\kappa}
factors through
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
since
R\subseteq N
and
[R,F_{2n}]\subseteq[N,F_{2n}]
. Because
\varpi\circ\widetilde{\kappa}=\rho\circ\pi
,
\widetilde{\kappa}
induces a surjection
\widetilde{\rho}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\to\widetilde{H}
such that
\varpi\circ\widetilde{\rho}=\rho\circ\pi_{n}
, which proves the existence of
\widetilde{\rho}
in the statement (
1
).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}

Without loss of generality, we assume
\xi_{n}
is the image of
[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1},x_{2n}]
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
. Then for any
\widetilde{\rho}
satisfying the desired requirments, we have that
\widetilde{\rho}
maps the image of
x_{i}
to
\widetilde{h}_{i}\delta_{i}
for some
\delta_{i}\in\ker\varpi
. However,
\ker\varpi
is in the center of
\widetilde{H}
, so
[\widetilde{h}_{1},\widetilde{h}_{2}]\cdots[\widetilde{h}_{2n-1},\widetilde{h}_{2n}]=[\widetilde{h}_{1}\delta_{1},\widetilde{h}_{2}\delta_{2}]\cdots[\widetilde{h}_{2n-1}\delta_{2n-1},\widetilde{h}_{2n}\delta_{2n}]
. Thus,
\widetilde{\rho}(\xi_{n})
does not depend on the choice of
\widetilde{\rho}
, which finishes the proof of (
1
).

Let
\rho_{i}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, be as defined in the statement (
2
). We use the surjections from
F_{2n}
to
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
defined in (
5.7
) and (
5.8
). Taking compositions with
\rho_{i}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}
, we obtain surjections
\kappa_{i}:F_{2n}\to H
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{i}:F_{2n}\to\widetilde{H}
such that
\varpi\circ\widetilde{\kappa}_{i}=\kappa_{i}
for each
i
. For an ordered
2n
-tuple
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})\in F_{2n}^{2n}
, we denote
\lambda(\mathbf{z}):=[z_{1},z_{2}]\cdots[z_{2n-1},z_{2n}]
. We write
\mathbf{x}
for the tuple of generators
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n})
. We will prove the statement (
2
) in the lemma by proving the following claim.

Claim: There exists
\theta\in\Aut(F_{2n})
such that
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}\circ\theta
and
\lambda(\theta(\mathbf{x}))=\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}
. Here
\theta(\mathbf{x})
denotes the tuple
(\theta(x_{1}),\ldots,\theta(x_{2n}))
.

We now explain how the claim implies the statement (
2
). In the presentation (
5.7
) of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, the kernel
R
is the closed normal subgroup generated by
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
. So if
\lambda(\theta(\mathbf{x}))=\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}
, then
R
is also generated by
\lambda(\theta(\mathbf{x}))
as
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\times}
. It follows that
\theta(R)=R
, and hence
\theta
induces
\varphi\in\Aut({\mathcal{G}}_{n})
, and also
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
by the description of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
in (
5.8
). The automorphisms
\varphi
and
\widetilde{\varphi}
satisfy the condition (
2a
) because
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}\circ\theta
and
\kappa_{i}
factors through
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}
and
\rho_{i}
for each
i=1,2
. And the condition (
2b
) is satisfied, because
\xi_{n}
is assumed to be the image of
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
and then
\widetilde{\varphi}(\xi_{n})
is the image of
\lambda(\theta(\mathbf{x}))
.

In the remainder of the proof, we will prove the claim by induction on the lower
\ell
-central series
\{F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\}
of
F_{2n}
, which is defined as

F_{2n}^{\{1\}}=F_{2n}\quad\text{and}\quad F_{2n}^{\{n+1\}}:=[F_{2n},F_{2n}^{\{n\}}](F_{2n}^{\{n\}})^{\ell}.

Explicitly, we will show the following.

(
\ast
):

For each
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>1}
, there exists a tuple
\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}}=(y_{1}^{\{j\}},\ldots,y_{2n}^{\{j\}})
formed by a set of generators
\{y_{i}^{\{j\}}\}_{i=1}^{2n}
of
F_{2n}
such that
\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}})\equiv\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}\mod F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}
and
\kappa_{1}(x_{i})=\kappa_{2}(y_{i}^{\{j\}})
for each
i=1,\ldots,2n
. Moreover,
y_{i}^{\{j+1\}}\equiv y_{i}^{\{j\}}\mod F_{2n}^{\{j\}}
.

Then the map sending
x_{i}
to the inverse limit of
\{y_{i}^{\{j\}}\mod F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\}
as
j\to\infty
defines an automorphism
\theta
satisfying all conditions in the claim.

Lemma 5.7
.

For
j=2
, the tuple
\mathbf{y}^{\{2\}}
as in (
\ast
) exists.

Proof.

Note that
F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}}
is the Frattini quotient of
F_{2n}
, so it is the dual of
H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
. The element
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
defines a symplectic pairing on
H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})\simeq H^{1}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-,-):H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})\times H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tr_{\lambda(\mathbf{x})}}

where
\tr_{\lambda(\mathbf{x})}
is the trace map induced by
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
. For each
r=1,2
, the surjection
\kappa_{r}
induces inflation map
\kappa_{r}^{*}:H^{1}(H,{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})\hookrightarrow H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
, and then we obtain a bilinear and alternating pairing on
H^{1}(H,{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
.
By the assumption that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}(\lambda(\mathbf{x}))=\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(\lambda(\mathbf{x}))
has order dividing
q-1
and the argument similar to Proposition
3.6
, the two pairings defined by
\kappa_{1}^{*}
and
\kappa_{2}^{*}
are the same and
(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})=q(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})
for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in H^{1}(H,{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
.
So we can choose a basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{a+b},f_{1},\ldots,f_{a}
of
H^{1}(H,{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
for some
a,b\geq 0
, such that
(e_{i},e_{j})=(f_{i},f_{j})=0
for all
i,j
and
(e_{i},f_{j})=1
if
i=j
and
0
otherwise. By the theory of symplectic space, we can extend this basis to a symplectic basis
e_{1,1},\ldots,e_{n,1},f_{1,1},\ldots,f_{n,1}
of
H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
via the embedding
\kappa_{1}^{*}
: that is, a basis such that
(e_{i,1},f_{i,1})=1
and all the other pairings of elements in the basis are zero, and moreover

e_{i,1}=\kappa_{1}^{*}(e_{i})
and
f_{j,1}=\kappa_{1}^{*}(f_{j})
for
1\leq i\leq a+b
and
1\leq j\leq a
. Similarly, we extend this basis to a
q
-symplectic basis
e_{1,2},\ldots,e_{n,2},f_{1,2},\ldots,f_{n,2}
of
H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
via the embedding
\kappa_{2}^{*}
such that
(e_{i,1},f_{i,1})=q
for all
i
, and
e_{i,2}=\kappa_{2}^{*}(e_{i})
and
f_{j,2}=\kappa_{2}^{*}(f_{j})
for
1\leq i\leq a+b
and
1\leq j\leq a
.

Then the automorphism
\beta\in\Aut(H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}))
sending
e_{i,1}\mapsto e_{i,2}
and
f_{i,1}\mapsto f_{i,2}
satisfies
\beta\circ\kappa_{1}^{*}=\kappa_{2}^{*}
and
(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})=q(\beta(\alpha),\beta(\alpha^{\prime}))
for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
. So by taking the dual, we obtain
\beta^{\vee}\in\Aut(F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}})
making the following diagram commute, where
\kappa_{r}^{\{2\}}
is the quotient map of the Frattini quotients induced by
\kappa_{r}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H/H^{\{2\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{2}^{\{2\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{1}^{\{2\}}}

Consider the fiber product
P:=H\times_{H/H^{\{2\}}}F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}}
defined by the natural surjection
H\to H/H^{\{2\}}
and
\kappa_{2}^{\{2\}}
. For each
i=1,\ldots,2n
, we let
z_{i}
denote the element in
P
whose first coordinate is
\kappa_{1}(x_{i})
and the second coordinate is the preimage under
\beta^{\vee}
of the image of
x_{i}
in
F_{2n}/F_{2n}^{\{2\}}
(these two coordinates have the same image in
H/H^{\{2\}}
because of the diagram above, so
z_{i}
is a well-defined element in
P
). Since
\kappa_{2}
factors through a surjection
\kappa_{2}^{\wedge}:F_{2n}\to P
obtained by quotienting
F_{2n}
by
\ker\kappa_{2}\cap F_{2n}^{\{2\}}
, by
[
24
, Lem.2.1]
, there exist elements
y_{1},\ldots,y_{2n}
in
F_{2n}
that generate
F_{2n}
and
\kappa_{2}^{\wedge}(y_{i})=z_{i}
. It follows that
\kappa_{1}(x_{i})=\kappa_{2}(y_{i})
and
\beta^{\vee}
sends the image of
y_{i}
to the image of
x_{i}
. Moreover, letting
\chi_{i}
and
\chi^{\prime}_{i}
denote the dual of
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
respectively in
H^{1}(F_{2n},{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell})
, we have
\beta(\chi_{i})=\chi^{\prime}_{i}
, and hence
(\chi_{i},\chi_{j})=q(\chi^{\prime}_{i},\chi^{\prime}_{j})
. Therefore, by
[
28
, Prop.(3.9.13)(ii)]
, we have
\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}\equiv[y_{1},y_{2}]\cdots[y_{2n-1},y_{2n}]\mod F_{2n}^{\{3\}}
, so the tuple
\mathbf{y}^{\{2\}}:=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{2n})
satisfies (
\ast
) for
j=2
.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Suppose that there exists
\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}}
in (
\ast
) for some
j>1
. Then
\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}}
exists.

Proof.

We will prove that there exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{2n}\in F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\cap\ker\kappa_{2}
such that the tuple
\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}}
obtained by setting
y_{i}^{\{j+1\}}
to be
y_{i}^{\{j\}}t_{i}
satisfies (
\ast
) . First, it is automatic that
y_{i}^{\{j+1\}}\equiv y_{i}^{\{j\}}
mod
F_{2n}^{\{j\}}
and
\kappa_{1}(x_{i})=\kappa_{2}(y_{i}^{\{j+1\}})
as
t_{i}\in F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\cap\ker\kappa_{2}
. So it suffices to consider the requirement
\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}})\equiv\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}\mod F_{2n}^{\{j+2\}}
. Let’s denote
D:=\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{-q}\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}})
. Since
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
and
\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}})
are both contained in
[F_{2n},F_{2n}]
, we have
D\in F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}\cap[F_{2n},F_{2n}]
.
Also, assuming that
\xi_{n}
is the image of
\lambda(\mathbf{x})
, we have

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q})=\widetilde{\rho}_{2}(\xi_{n})^{q}=\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(\xi_{n})=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}(\lambda(\mathbf{x}))=\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}})),

where the first and the third equalities follow by definition of
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
, the second one follows by the assumption in the proposition that
\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(\xi_{n})=\widetilde{\rho}_{2}(\xi_{n})
has order dividing
q-1
, and the last one is because of the statement (
1
) in the proposition and the induction hypothesis
\kappa_{1}(x_{i})=\kappa_{2}(y_{i}^{\{j\}})
. So we have that
D
is contained in
F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}\cap[F_{2n},F_{2n}]\cap\ker\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
.

The lower
\ell
-central series
\{F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\}
induces a Lie algebra
\gr(F_{2n}):=\sum_{i}\gr_{i}(F_{2n})
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
, where
\gr_{i}(F_{2n}):=F_{2n}^{\{j\}}/F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}
and the Lie bracket for homogenous elements of
\gr(F_{2n})
is induced by the commutator: if
a=\overline{x}\in\gr_{r}(F_{2n})
and
b=\overline{y}\in\gr_{s}(F_{2n})
, then
[a,b]
is the image of the commutator
[x,y]
in
\gr_{r+s}(F_{2n})
. So

\displaystyle\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[y_{1}^{\{j+1\}},y_{2}^{\{j+1\}}]\cdots[y_{2n-1}^{\{j+1\}},y_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[y_{1}^{\{j\}}t_{1},y_{2}^{\{j\}}t_{2}]\cdots[y_{2n-1}^{\{j\}}t_{2n-1},y_{2n}^{\{j\}}t_{2n}]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j\}})[t_{1},y_{2}^{\{j\}}][y_{1}^{\{j\}},t_{2}]\cdots[t_{2n-1},y_{2n}^{\{j\}}][y_{2n-1}^{\{j\}},t_{2n}]\mod F_{2n}^{\{j+2\}}.

We write the group action of
\gr_{i}(F_{2n})
additively, and define

d_{j}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{2n}):=[\tau_{1},\sigma_{2}]+[\tau_{2},\sigma_{1}]+\cdots+[\tau_{2n-1},\sigma_{2n}]+[\tau_{2n},\sigma_{2n-1}]\in\gr_{j+1}(F_{2n}),

where
\tau_{i}
is the image of
t_{i}
in
\gr_{j}(F_{2n})
and
\sigma_{i}
is the image of
y_{i}^{\{j\}}
in
\gr_{1}(F_{2n})
.

Considering the short exact sequence
1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow F_{2n}\overset{\kappa_{2}}{\longrightarrow}H\longrightarrow 1
defined by
\kappa_{2}
, by
[
16
, Chap.11, Thm.2.3(iii) and (iv)(b)]
,
[N,F_{2n}]
is
[F_{2n},F_{2n}]\cap\ker\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}
. So, the image of
F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}\cap[F_{2n},F_{2n}]\cap\ker\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}=F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}\cap[N,F_{2n}]
in
\gr_{j+1}(F_{2n})
is generated by
[a,b]
for some
a\in\gr_{r}(F_{2n})
and
b\in\gr_{j+1-r}(F_{2n})
such that
a
is the image of some element of
N
. If
b=c^{\ell}
for some
c\in\gr_{j-r}(F_{2n})
, then
[a,b]=[a^{\ell},c]
and
a^{\ell}
is the image in
\gr_{r+1}(F_{2n})
of some element of
N
. If
b=[c,d]
, then
[a,b]=[[d,a],c]+[[a,c],d]
and each of
[d,a]
and
[a,c]
is the image of some element of
N
and of degree strictly larger than
r
. It follows that the image of
F_{2n}^{\{j+1\}}\cap[N,F_{2n}]
in
\gr_{j+1}(F_{2n})
is generated by
[a,b]
where
a\in\gr_{j}(F_{2n})
,
b\in\gr_{1}(F_{2n})
, and
a
is the image of some element of
N
. Because
y_{1}^{\{j\}},\ldots,y_{2n}^{\{j\}}
form a generating set of
F_{2n}
, we have that
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{2n}
generate
\gr_{1}(F_{2n})
, and hence the argument above shows that there exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{2n}\in F_{2n}^{\{j\}}\cap\ker\kappa_{2}
such that
d_{j}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{2n})
equals the image of
D
in
\gr_{j+1}(F_{2n})
. Then for such a choice of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{2n}
, we have the tuple
\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}}
satisfying
\lambda(\mathbf{y}^{\{j+1\}})\equiv\lambda(\mathbf{x})^{q}
, and hence we finish the proof.
∎

6.
Conjectures and Evidences

In §
6.1
, we give the statements of all of the conjectures made in this paper, which include the moment conjectures in both the function field case and the number field case, and also the conjectures regarding the existence and uniqueness of the probability measure with desired moments. Then in §
6.2
and §
6.3
, we discuss the presentation of
G_{\O}(K)
and positively ramifications, and how they support the moment conjecture in the number field case.

6.1.
Statements of the conjectures

We first recall the notion of pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion of a
\Gamma
-group defined in
[
21
]
.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a set of isomorphism classes of finite
\Gamma
-groups, and define
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
to be the smallest set of isomorphism classes of
\Gamma
-groups containing
{\mathcal{C}}
that is closed under taking finite direct product,
\Gamma
-quotient and
\Gamma
-subgroup. Then for a given profinite
\Gamma
-group
G
, the
pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion of
G
is defined to be

G^{{\mathcal{C}}}=\varprojlim_{M}G/M,

where the inverse limit runs over all closed normal
\Gamma
-subgroup
M
of
G
such that
G/M\in\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}
. We say that a group
G
is
pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
if
G=G^{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Let
q
be a positive integer such that
\gcd(q,|\Gamma|)=1
.
We consider pairs
(H,h)
for a profinite admissible
q^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-group
H
and an element
h\in H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[q-1]
. We say that two such pairs
(G,g)
and
(H,h)
are isomorphic if there exists an
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism
G\simeq H
such that its induced coinflation map
H_{2}(G\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
sends
g\mapsto h
.
We define a topological space
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, whose underlying space is the set of isomorphism classes of pairs
(H,h)
as described above and basic opens are the sets

U_{{\mathcal{C}},H,h}:=\left\{(G,g)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}\,\Bigg|\,\begin{aligned} &\text{there is a $\Gamma$-equivariant surjection $e:G\to H$ s.t. }\\
&\text{$e$ induces an isom. $G^{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq H$ and the coinf map sending $g\mapsto h$}\end{aligned}\right\},

for each finite set
{\mathcal{C}}
of finite
(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups and each pair
(H,h)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
such that
H
is finite pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
.
For a measure
\mu
on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, we use the following notation to denote the average number of surjections to a pair
(H,h)

{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h)):=\sum_{(G,g)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(G,H)\,\bigg|\,\begin{aligned} &\text{the $\coinf$ map induced }\\
&\text{by $\pi$ sends $g\mapsto h$}\end{aligned}\right\}{\mu}(G,g).

Conjecture 6.1

(Existence and uniqueness of the limit probability measure with the desired moments)
.

For a finite group
\Gamma
and a positive integer
q
satisfying
\gcd(q,|\Gamma|)=1
, there exists a unique probability measure
\mu_{\Gamma,q}
on the topological space
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, such that for each
(H,h)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, the following equality holds

{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{\Gamma,q}}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h))=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]}.

When
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
, we’ve discussed in Section
5
that there is a random pro-
\ell
group model
Y_{q,n}
giving the desired moments. Next, we define a coarser topological space
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q,\ell}
on which our pro-
\ell
version of the probability measure is defined, and then we conjecture that the limit of the random group model
Y_{q,n}
as
n\to\infty
gives the desired probability measure.

Definition 6.2

(Definition of measures
\nu_{q,n,\ell}
on pro-
\ell

{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-groups)
.

Let
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\ell
an odd prime.
We define a coarser topology
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q,\ell}
on the underlying space of
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
: where the basic opens are all
U_{{\mathcal{C}},H,h}
with an additional condition that
H
is an
\ell
-group.
For fixed
q,n
satisfying
\gcd(2\ell,q)=1
, we use the notation in Theorem
5.6
, fix a generator
\xi_{n}
of
H_{2}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)
, and define a probability measure
\nu_{q,\ell,n}
on the space
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q,\ell}
by

\nu_{q,\ell,n}(U_{{\mathcal{C}},H,h}):=\Prob\left(\begin{aligned} &\text{there exists $\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(Y_{q,n},H)$ s.t. }\\
&\text{$\pi$ induces an isom. $Y^{{\mathcal{C}}}_{q,n}\simeq H$ and $\pi_{\dagger}(\xi_{n})=h$}\end{aligned}\right),

for each finite set
{\mathcal{C}}
of
\ell
-
\Gamma
-groups and pair
(H,h)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q,\ell}
such that
H
is finite pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Conjecture 6.3
.

When
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
, on the topological space
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q,\ell}
,
the probability measures
\nu_{q,\ell,n}
have weak convergence to the measure
\mu_{\Gamma,q}
in Conjecture
6.1
, as
n\to\infty
.

Next, we give the probability and moment conjecture in the function field case.

Conjecture 6.4

(Function field case)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group and
q
a prime power such that
\gcd(q,|\Gamma|)=1
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a finite set of finite
(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups, and
(H,h)
a pair in
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
such that
H
is finite pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
. Fix a generator
\zeta
of
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
, and we let
\delta
denote the homomorphism
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)_{(q|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}\to H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}
sending
\zeta\mapsto h
. Then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\#\left\{K\in E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))\,\bigg|\,\begin{aligned} &\text{ there exists $\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H)$ s.t.}\\
&\text{ $\pi$ induces an isom. $G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq H$ and $\pi_{*}\circ\omega_{K}^{\#}=\delta$}\end{aligned}\right\}}{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\#E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}=\mu_{\Gamma,q}(U_{{\mathcal{C}},H,h}).

and

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\sum\limits_{K\in E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}\#\left\{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}\left(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H\right)\,\bigg|\,\pi_{*}\circ\omega_{K}^{\#}=\delta\right\}}{\sum\limits_{n\leq N}\#E_{\Gamma}(q^{n},{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t))}=\frac{1}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]}.

To give the moment conjecture in the number field case, we need to first study how the action of taking quotient modulo
u
random elements changes the moments. This idea comes from
[
19
]
, and Lemma
6.6
below is the analogue of
[
19
, Lem.8.12]
for our notion of the probability space.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
\mu
be a measure on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
. For a positive integer
t
, define the measure
{\mathcal{Q}}^{t}\mu
on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
as obtained by taking the
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of the random pair
(H,h)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
according to
\mu
modulo
t
random elements. That is, for any
(H,h)
, define

{\mathcal{Q}}^{t}\mu(H,h):=\sum_{(G,g)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\Prob\left(G/[x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}]\simeq H\text{ such that }g\mapsto h\right)\mu(G,g),

where
x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}
are
t
random elements of
G
with respect to Haar measure, and the notation
[x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}]
represents the closed normal
\Gamma
-subgroup of
G
generated by
x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\mu
be a measure on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
. Then for any
(H,h)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{t}\mu}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h))=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h))}{|H|^{t}}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the lemma for
t=1
. For two pairs
(G_{1},g_{1}),(G_{2},g_{2})\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
, we write
\pi:(G_{1},g_{1})\to(G_{2},g_{2})
if
\pi
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant group homomorphism
G_{1}\to G_{2}
whose induced coinflation maps
g_{1}
to
g_{2}
, and we write
\Sur_{\Gamma}((G_{1},g_{1}),(G_{2},g_{2}))
for the set of surjections
(G_{1},g_{1})\to(G_{2},g_{2})
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}\mu}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(G,g)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}\left((G,g),(H,h)\right)\cdot{\mathcal{Q}}\mu(G,g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(G,g)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\sum_{(E,e)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}\left((G,g),(H,h)\right)\cdot\Prob\left(E/[x]\simeq G\text{ s.t. }e\mapsto g\right)\cdot\mu(E,e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(E,e)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\sum_{\pi\in\Sur_{\Gamma}\left((E,e),(H,h)\right)}\Prob\left(\text{the random $x\in E$ is contained in $\ker\pi$}\right)\cdot\mu(E,e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(E,e)\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}\left((E,e),(H,h)\right)\cdot\frac{1}{|H|}\cdot\mu(E,e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(*,(H,h))}{|H|}.

∎

Conjecture 6.7

(Number field case)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group,
Q
a number field, and
m
the maximal integer such that the
m
-th roots of unity
\mu_{m}
is contained in
Q
. Let
G_{\O}^{\#}(K):=G_{\O}(K)_{(|\Gamma||G_{\O}(Q)|)^{\prime}}
, and
H
be a finite admissible
\Gamma
-group such that
G_{\O}(Q)
is pro-prime-to
|H|
. Then, for any extension
K/Q
,

\lim_{B\to\infty}\frac{\sum_{D\leq B}\sum_{K\in E_{\Gamma}(D,Q)}\#\Sur_{\Gamma}(G^{\#}_{\O}(K),H)}{\sum_{D\leq B}\#E_{\Gamma}(D,Q)}=\frac{\#H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[m]}{[H:H^{\Gamma}]\cdot|H|^{u}},

where
u
is the rank of the group of units in the ring
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}
of algebraic integers of
Q
.

Remark 6.8
.

(1)

Although we will see in §
6.3
that, for some number field
K
, we can define a lifting invariant that is analogous to the invariant
\omega_{K}^{\#}
in the function field case, in general, we don’t know how to define such an invariant, so in Conjecture
6.7
, we do not specify the “lifting invariant” and study only the distribution of
G_{\O}^{\#}(K)
.

(2)

We only make the conjecture in the moment version because in the number field case the characteristic is zero and then the distribution of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is not a measure on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\Gamma,q}
for any
q
. However, one can similarly define a topology on the set of pairs
(H,h)
where
H
is an admissible
\Gamma
-group and
h\in H_{2}(H\rtimes\Gamma,{\mathbb{Z}})_{(|\Gamma|)^{\prime}}[m]
, and conjecture that there exists a unique probability measure on this topological space that gives the desired moments.

6.2.
Evidence I: Presentations of
G_{\O}(K)

In this section, we fix a finite group
\Gamma
and let
Q
denote a global field that is either
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
or a number field.

We let
F_{n}(\Gamma)
denote
the free
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-group on
n
generators

[
21
, §3.1]
, that is:
F_{n}(\Gamma)
is the free pro-
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
group on
\{x_{i,\gamma}\mid i=1,\ldots n\text{ and }\gamma\in\Gamma\}
where
\sigma\in\Gamma
acts on
F_{n}(\Gamma)
by
\sigma(x_{i,\gamma})=x_{i,\sigma\gamma}
. Let
G
be a finitely generated
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-group. For a sufficiently large
n
, there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant short exact sequence of
\Gamma
-groups

1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow F_{n}(\Gamma)\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}G\longrightarrow 1,

and we call such a short exact sequence
a
\Gamma
-presentation of
G
. For a set
{\mathcal{C}}
of isomorphism classes of
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups, if
G
is pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
, then the map
\pi
factors through the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion map
F_{n}(\Gamma)\to F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
. We call the induced short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\overset{\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\longrightarrow}G\longrightarrow 1

a pro-
{\mathcal{C}}

\Gamma
-presentation of
G
.

For the rest of this section, we assume
{\mathcal{C}}
is finite. For any
\Gamma
-extension
K
of
Q
that is split completely at
\infty
or at all archimedean primes, by
[
23
, Thm.6.4]
, the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completion of
G_{\O}(K)
is finite, so
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
is finite because
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is a quotient of
G_{\O}(K)
in both the number field case and the function field case. Then we can study a pro-
{\mathcal{C}}

\Gamma
-presentation

1\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\overset{\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\longrightarrow}G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow 1.

(6.1)

The work
[
23
]
studies the minimal number of elements that generates
M
as a
\Gamma
-closed normal subgroup of
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
in such a presentation, and gives an upper bound for this minimal number using Galois cohomology groups
H^{i}(G_{\O}(K),A)
for
i=1,2
and
A
a finite simple
\Gal(K_{\O}/Q)
-module. We will apply that result to number field case and function field case respectively, and show that the number field case needs
u
more elements than the function field case to generate
M
, where
u
is the rank of
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}^{\times}
as defined in Conjecture
6.7
.

We first recall the definition of multiplicities in
[
23
, Def.3.1]
. Given a short exact sequence
1\to N\to F\overset{\omega}{\longrightarrow}H\to 1
of
\Gamma
-groups, we let
N_{0}
be the intersection of all maximal proper
F\rtimes\Gamma
-normal subgroups of
N
, and denote
\overline{N}=N/N_{0}
and
\overline{F}=F/N_{0}
. Then
\overline{N}
is a direct sum of finite irreducible
\overline{F}\rtimes\Gamma
-groups. For any finite irreducible
\overline{F}\rtimes\Gamma
-group
A
, we define the
multiplicity
m(\omega,\Gamma,H,A)
to be the multiplicity of
A
appearing in
\overline{N}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
Q
be
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
with
\gcd(q,|\Gamma|)=1
or a number field. Let
K
be a
\Gamma
-extension of
Q
such that each archimedean prime is unramified when
Q
is a number field, and
\infty
splits completely when
Q={\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a finite set of
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups when
Q={\mathbb{F}}_{q}(t)

, and a finite set of
(|\Gamma||G_{\O}(Q)|)^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups when
Q
is a number field. Let
\ell
be a prime such that
\ell\neq\Char(Q)
,
\ell\nmid|\Gamma|
, and
\ell\nmid|G_{\O}(Q)|
when
Q
is a number field.
We consider a pro-
{\mathcal{C}}

\Gamma
-presentation (
6.1
) of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Then for a finite simple
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}[G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma]
-module
A
, we have

m(\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)\leq\begin{cases}\dfrac{(n+1)\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A-\xi(A)-{\bf{1}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}(A)}{\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A)}&\text{ if $Q$ is a function field}\\
\dfrac{(n+u+1)\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A-\xi(A)-{\bf{1}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}(A)}{\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A)}&\text{ if $Q$ is a number field}.\end{cases}

Here
\xi(A):=\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A^{\Gamma}/A^{G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}
;
{\bf{1}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}(A)
is 1 if
A={\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
and
\mu_{\ell}\not\subset Q
, and 0 otherwise; and
u
is the rank of
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}^{\times}
.

Remark 6.10
.

We take the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completions for a finite
{\mathcal{C}}
only because we want to work with a presentation with finitely many generators. If
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is finitely generated, then the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}
completions in the inequality can be removed. Also, we can take an ascending series of finite sets
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\}
such that
\cup\,{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
contains all isomorphism classes of
|\Gamma|^{\prime}
-
\Gamma
-groups, then
\varprojlim G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}=G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
. We will see in the discussion after the proof of this lemma that only the difference between the upper bounds in the number field case and the function field case is important, which does not depend on the choice of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

We denote
G:=G_{\O}(K)
and
G^{\#}:=G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
.
By
[
23
, Prop.3.4 and Cor.5.3]
,

m(\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,(G^{\#})^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)\leq\frac{n\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A-\xi(A)+\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{2}(G^{\#},A)^{\Gamma}-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{1}(G^{\#},A)^{\Gamma}}{\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{(G^{\#})^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A)}

(6.2)

where the
\Gamma
actions on the cohomology groups are induced by the conjugation action of
\Gamma
on
G^{\#}
.

We first prove the function field case. Note that
G^{\#}
is the quotient of
G
modulo the decomposition subgroup at
\infty
. So we can assume that
n
is sufficiently large such that
\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}}
is the composition map
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\overset{\hat{\pi}^{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\longrightarrow}G^{{\mathcal{C}}}\to(G^{\#})^{{\mathcal{C}}}
. Then we have a formula for
m(\hat{\pi}^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,G^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)
similar to (
6.2
). Because
K/Q
is split completely above
\infty
, the genus of
K
is positive. By
[
23
, Prop.9.3(2)]
,

\displaystyle\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{2}(G,A)^{\Gamma}-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{1}(G,A)^{\Gamma}

(6.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{\Gal(K_{\O}/Q)}(A,\mu_{\ell})-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A^{\Gal(K_{\O}/Q)}.

Here
A
is a
(G^{\#})^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma
-module, and hence is a
\Gal(K^{\#}_{\O}/Q)
-module. Because
K^{\#}_{\O}/Q
is split completely over
\infty
, if
A=\mu_{\ell}
, then
\mu_{\ell}
must be contained in
Q
, so
A
is a trivial module and (
6.3
) is
1-1=0
. If
A={\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
, then (
6.3
) is
-1
when
\mu_{\ell}\not\subset Q
and is 0 otherwise. If
A
is neither
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
or
\mu_{\ell}
, then both terms in (
6.3
) are 0. So we have an upper bound:
m(\hat{\pi}^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,G^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)\leq(n\dim A-\xi(A)-{\bf 1}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}(A))\cdot(\dim\Hom_{G^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A))^{-1}
. Recall that
G^{\#}
is the
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of
G
modulo one element (a generator of the decomposition at
\infty
). By following the proof of
[
23
, Lem.10.6]
, we have
m(\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,(G^{\#})^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)=m(\hat{\pi}^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,G^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)+\dim A\cdot(\dim\Hom_{G^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A))^{-1}
, and hence we prove the lemma in the function field case.

When
Q
is a number field,
G=G^{\#}
because of the assumption that
{\mathcal{C}}
contains only groups whose order are prime to
|\Gamma||G_{\O}(Q)|
. Let
T
denote the set of all archimedean primes and all primes of
K
above
\ell
. Let
S_{\infty}(Q)
denote the set of all archimedean primes of
Q
. By
[
23
, Thm.7.1]
,

\displaystyle\log_{\ell}\chi_{K/Q,T}(A)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}(-1)^{i}\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{i}(G_{T}(K),A)^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in S_{\infty}(Q)}\left(\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\widehat{H}^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{\ell}))-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{\ell}))\right),

where
\widehat{H}^{0}
is the 0-th Tate cohomology of the group
G_{Q_{v}}
.
We let
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
denote the number of real embeddings and complex embeddings of
Q
respectively.
If
v
is imaginary, then
\dim H^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{\ell}))=\dim\Hom(A,\mu_{\ell})=\dim A
.
When
\ell
is odd,
G_{Q_{v}}
is either trivial or
\Gal({\mathbb{C}}/{\mathbb{R}})={\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
, so the Tate cohomology terms above are always 0
[
28
, Prop.(1.6.2)]
.
Since
K_{\O}/Q
is unramified at every archimedean prime, if
v
is real, then
A
as a
G_{Q_{v}}
-module is trivial but
\mu_{\ell}
is not trivial, so
H^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{\ell}))=\Hom_{G_{Q_{v}}}(A,\mu_{\ell})=0
.
When
\ell=2
, for each real place
v
of
Q
,
G_{Q_{v}}
acts trivially on
A
and so on
\Hom(A,\mu_{2})
. Since
\Hom(A,\mu_{2})
has exponent 2, its
G_{Q_{v}}
-norm map is zero, which implies that
\widehat{H}^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{2}))=H^{0}(Q_{v},\Hom(A,\mu_{2}))
. When
\ell=2
and
v
is imaginary,
G_{Q_{v}}=1
, so the Tate cohomology term is trivial. Therefore, in every case, we have
\log_{\ell}\chi_{K/Q,T}(A)=-r_{2}\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A
.

Then by
[
23
, Prop.9.4]
,

\displaystyle\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{2}(G,A)^{\Gamma}-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}H^{1}(G,A)^{\Gamma}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-r_{2}\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A+\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{\Gal(K_{T}/Q)}(A,\mu_{\ell})-\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A^{\Gal(K_{\O}/Q)}+[K:{\mathbb{Q}}]\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A.

Similarly to the function field case, if
A=\mu_{\ell}
, then
\mu_{\ell}\subset Q
and
\Delta:=\dim\Hom_{\Gal(K_{T}/Q)}(A,\mu_{\ell})-\dim A^{\Gal(K_{\O}/Q)}=0
. If
A
is neither
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
or
\mu_{\ell}
, then
\Delta=0
. When
A={\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
,
\Delta
is
0
if
\mu_{\ell}\subset Q
and is
-1
when
\mu_{\ell}\not\subset Q
. Finally, because
[K:{\mathbb{Q}}]=r_{1}+2r_{2}
and
u=r_{1}+r_{2}-1
, the lemma in the number field case follows by (
6.4
).
∎

The multiplicities computed in Lemma
6.9
are closely related to the minimal number of relators in the presentation (
6.1
) (which is the minimal number of elements that generate
M
as a
\Gamma
-closed normal subgroup of
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
). Let
R
and
F
be the quotients of
M
and
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
respectively modulo the intersection of all maximal proper
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma
-normal subgroups of
M
. Then

R\simeq\bigoplus_{A}A^{m(\pi^{{\mathcal{C}}},\Gamma,G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}},A)},

where the direct sum runs over all finite simple
F\rtimes\Gamma
-groups
A
. Then a set of elements generates
M
as a
\Gamma
-closed normal subgroup of
F_{n}(\Gamma)^{{\mathcal{C}}}
if and only if the image of the set in
R
generates
R
as a
\Gamma
-closed normal subgroup of
F
. By
[
22
, Thm.5.1 and Rmk.5.2]
, for a non-abelian
A
, the direct product of arbitrarily many copies of
A
can always be generated by 1 element; and for an abelian
A
, the maximal
m
such that
A^{m}
can be generated by 1 element is
\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}A\cdot(\dim_{{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}}\Hom_{G^{\#}_{\O}(K)^{{\mathcal{C}}}\rtimes\Gamma}(A))^{-1}
. Thus, the upper bounds for the multiplicities given in Lemma
6.9
show that in the pro-
{\mathcal{C}}

\Gamma
-presentation (
6.1
), the number field case needs
u
more relators than the function field case. If these
u
extra relators are random, then Conjecture
6.7
follows by Conjecture
6.4
and Lemma
6.6
(regardless of the “prime-to-
q
” condition in the function field case).

Of course, in Lemma
6.9
, we give only upper bounds of the multiplicities, and if there exist better upper bounds, then one may wonder whether Conjecture
6.7
is still reasonable.
[
23
]
shows that the equality in Lemma
6.9
holds if and only if, in the number field case,
H^{2}(G_{\O}(K),A)
and
\B_{\O}(K,A)
are in the same class of the Grothendieck group of
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K/Q)]
-modules (see the proof of
[
23
, Prop.9.4]
). Here
\B_{\O}(K,A)
is an abelian group defined in
[
23
, Def.8.1]
.
We don’t know to what extent this condition holds, so it’s unclear whether there are upper bounds that are generically better than the ones given in Lemma
6.9
.

6.3.
Evidence II: Positively ramified extensions of number fields

If Conjectures
6.4
and
6.7
are true, then one may expect that, for a number field
K
, there exists some group associated to
K
such that: 1) it has group structure similar to the prime-to-
\Char(k)
part of the étale fundamental group of a smooth projective curve
X
over a finite field
k
; and 2)
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient of that group modulo
u
elements. In the function field case, for
K
satisfying Notation
2.1
, we have seen that
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
is naturally a quotient of
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X_{\overline{k}})=G_{\O}(K\overline{k})
. When we focus on the pro-
\ell
completions of Galois groups in the number field case, then the passage to the algebraic closure of constant field is analogous to taking the composition of
K
with the cyclotomic
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-extension
Q_{\infty}
of
Q
. However, the étale fundamental group
G_{\O}(K)
is too small to be an analogue of
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X)
because
Q_{\infty}
is already ramified. Let
S
be a set of primes of
K
that contains all the archimedean primes and primes above
\ell
, and let
G_{S}(K)(\ell)
denote the Galois group of the maximal pro-
\ell
extension of
K
that is unramified outside primes above
S
. It is well known that there exists a strong analogy between
G_{S}(K)(\ell)
and
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X-\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}\})(\ell)
when
\ell\neq\Char(k)
: both of them are Poincaré group at
\ell
of dimension 2 and have the Poitou–Tate duality. Because the Riemann existence theorem shows that there is a natural surjection

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{\et}(X-\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}\})(\ell)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{\et}(X)(\ell)}

whose kernel is the free product of the inertia subgroups at
P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}
. So if the analogue of
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X)(\ell)
exists, then it should be some group between
G_{S}(K)(\ell)\twoheadrightarrow G_{\O}(K)(\ell)
.

In the sequence of works by Wingberg and Schmidt
[
34
,
35
,
32
,
33
]
, they introduced the notion of positively ramified extensions of number fields, and in some nice cases, they prove that there is a good analogy between the Galois group of the maximal positively ramified extension and
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X)
. We briefly explain their ideas below.
Let
\ell
be an odd prime number, and
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}:={\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{nr}(\zeta_{\ell}+\zeta_{\ell}^{-1})(odd)(\zeta_{\ell})
, where
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{nr}
is the maximal unramified extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and the notation
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{nr}(\zeta_{\ell}+\zeta_{\ell}^{-1})(odd)
means the maximal pro-odd extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{nr}(\zeta_{\ell}+\zeta_{\ell}^{-1})
. So
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{nr}(\zeta_{\ell}+\zeta_{\ell}^{-1})(odd)
, and we let
\rho
denote the non-trivial element of its Galois group, which can be viewed as the
\ell
-adic version of the complex conjugation. A local field
k/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is
orientable
if
k\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}
and if either
k=k^{+}:=k^{\langle{\rho}\rangle}
or
\zeta_{\ell}\in k^{nr}
. In other words, at each local place, Wingberg and Schmidt construct the structure that is similar to a CM-field. So the positively ramified extension is the analogue of the totally real field: a local extension
l/k
is
positively ramified (p.r.)
if
l\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}k
(in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{2}
if
p=2
) and the Galois closure of
l/k
has at most pure wild ramification (i.e., the ramification index is a power of
\ell
). Then a number field extension
L/K
is called
positively ramified (p.r.)
if its local completion is p.r. at each prime.

For an odd prime number
\ell
, a number field
K
is called
\ell
-CM
if, at every prime
{\mathfrak{p}}
of
K
lying above
\ell
,
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is orientable,
[K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}]=2
and
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
contains an index-2 subfield
k
such that
k=k^{+}
. We let
K_{\pos}
denote the maximal p.r. extension of
K
. Then by
[
33
, Thm.7.3]
, if
K
is
\ell
-CM and contains
\mu_{\ell}
, then the followings hold (for analogous statements for
\pi_{1}^{\et}(X)
, see
[
28
, Cor.(10.1.3)(i)(ii)]
):

(1)

If
g_{\ell}(K)>0
, then
\Gal(K_{\pos}/K)(\ell)
is a Poincaré group of dimension 3 with dualizing module
\mu_{\ell^{\infty}}
.

(2)

If
g_{\ell}(K)=0
, then
\Gal(K_{\pos}/K)(\ell)
is a free pro-
\ell
group.

Here
g_{\ell}(K)
is the
\ell
-genus
which can be computed by the rank of the positive ramified Picard group
[
33
, Prop.6.7]
. Moreover, Schmidt
[
33
, Thm.8.3]
proved the analogue of the Riemann existence theorem: kernel of
G_{S}(K)(\ell)\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K_{\pos}/K)(\ell)
is the free product of inertia subgroups.

Assume that
\ell
is an odd prime number,
Q={\mathbb{Q}}(\mu_{\ell})
such that
Q_{\O}(\ell)=Q
(equivalently, the
\ell
-part of the class group of
Q
is trivial), and
\Gamma
is a finite group with
\ell\nmid|\Gamma|
. Then
Q
is
\ell
-CM. Assume that
K
is a
\Gamma
-extension of
Q
that is
\ell
-CM (for example, this holds when
K/Q
is unramified at all primes lying above
\ell
). We let
K_{\pos}(\ell)
denote the maximal pro-
\ell
p.r. extension of
K
. Then
KQ_{\infty}
is a p.r. extension of
K
, and we have the following short exact sequence that is the analogue of (
2.1
)

1\longrightarrow\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty})\longrightarrow\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/K)\longrightarrow\Gal(KQ_{\infty}/K)\longrightarrow 1,

(6.5)

where every term naturally has an action of
\Gamma
(when we fix a splitting of
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/Q)\to\Gal(K/Q)
).
The Galois group
\Gal(KQ_{\infty}/K)\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
, which is a Poincaré group of dimension 1 with dualizing module
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. So by
[
28
, Thm.(3.7.4)]
, from the exact sequence above, we see that
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty})
is a pro-
\ell
Poincaré group of dimension 2 with dualizing module
\mu_{\ell^{\infty}}
, and hence we have the analogue of Poincaré duality.
Moreover, since
\mu_{\ell^{\infty}}\subset KQ_{\infty}
, by Kummer theory, the abelianization of
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty})
is free over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
, so
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty})
is a pro-
\ell
Demuškin group in the form of
\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{2n}\mid[x_{1},x_{2}]\cdots[x_{2n-1},x_{2n}]\rangle
.
So one can repeat the argument in the proof of Lemma
2.2
to show that there is a
\Gal(Q_{\infty}/Q)
-equivariant isomorphism

H_{2}(\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/Q_{\infty}),{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1).

(6.6)

We let
v
denote the unique prime of
Q
lying above
\ell
, let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be a prime of
K
lying above
v
, and pick a prime
{\mathfrak{P}}
of
K_{\pos}(\ell)
above
{\mathfrak{p}}
. By
[
28
, Thm.(7.4.4)]
and the assumption
\ell\nmid|\Gamma|
, the group of principal units of
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is isomorphic to

U^{1}_{K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})]^{\ell-1}

as
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})
-modules. Let
a
denote a generator of
\Gal(KQ_{\infty}/K)
. The extension
KQ_{\infty}/K
is completely widely ramified at
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then by local class field theory, we can pick an element
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
of the inertia subgroup
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/K)
at
{\mathfrak{P}}
, such that it maps to
a
under the quotient map in (
6.5
) and the
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})
-closed subgroup of the abelianization of
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)_{{\mathfrak{P}}}/K_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
generated by the image of
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})]
. Then
for other prime
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
of
K
above
v
, we choose an element
\gamma\in\Gal(K/Q)
such that
\gamma({\mathfrak{p}})={\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
, and define
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
to be
\gamma(a_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
.
We define
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
to be the quotient of
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty})
modulo the
\Gamma
-closed normal subgroup generated by these elements
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
’s for all
{\mathfrak{p}}\mid v
, and we denote the field extension corresponding to
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
by
K^{\#}_{\pos}/KQ_{\infty}
. The group
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
is designed in the way such that it is a reasonable analogue of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
in Notation
2.1
. Indeed, we have to take the above approach to quotient out all the relevant lifts of
\Gal(KQ_{\infty}/K)
(which is the analogue of the Frobenius), because in the function field case the place
\infty
of
X_{\overline{k}}
cannot further ramify or be further inert.
For example, if
v
splits completely in
K/Q
, then the elements
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
’s are lifts of
a
that are compatible with the
\Gal(K/Q)
action on all the primes above
v
, which is exactly the same situation as the function field case.
So the quotient map
\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/Q_{\infty})\twoheadrightarrow\Gal(K_{\pos}^{\#}/Q_{\infty})
induces a coinflation map, which gives the analogue of the invariant
\omega_{K}^{\#}
in Definition
2.13
:

{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1)\simeq H_{2}(\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/Q_{\infty}),{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)\longrightarrow H_{2}(\Gal(K_{\pos}^{\#}/Q_{\infty}),{\mathbb{Z}})(\ell)

and it’s clear by our construction that this map factors through
\mu_{\ell}
.
We cannot recover the group theoretical description of the invariant as described in Proposition
2.6
and Corollary
2.15
because we don’t have the analogue of the system of inertia generators for
\Gamma
-extensions of
Q_{\infty}
. However, we can generalize the cohomological definition of the invariant in §
2.2
: we can define a trace map
H^{2}(\Gal(K_{\pos}(\ell)/KQ_{\infty}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}(1))\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
and choose the isomorphism (
6.6
) such that it corresponds to the class of trace
-|\Gamma|
.

By the discussion above, it’s reasonable to consider
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
for a number field
K
satisfying our assumptions as a good analogue of
G^{\#}_{\O}(K)
for a function field
K
. Then, for the number field case, we consider the natural quotient map

G^{\#}_{\pos}(K)\longrightarrow G_{\O}(K)(\ell),

(6.7)

whose kernel is the normal subgroup generated by the inertia subgroups of
K_{\pos}^{\#}/K
at primes above
v
. Let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be a prime of
K
above
v
, and
{\mathfrak{P}}
a prime of
K_{\pos}^{\#}
above
{\mathfrak{p}}
. By definition of p.r., the local completion
(K_{\pos}^{\#})_{{\mathfrak{P}}}/K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a sub-
\ell
-extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}/K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. As we assumed that
K
is
\ell
-CM,
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}
has index 2 in
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}/Q_{v}^{+})\cong\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})
. Recall that
\rho
is the
\ell
-complex conjucation, we let
(U_{K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1})^{+}
denote the
\rho
-invariant subgroup of the principal unit group
U_{K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1}
. Then since
U_{K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})]
as
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
-modules, we have the following isomorphism of
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})
-modules

(U_{K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{1})^{+}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})]\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{+}/Q_{v}^{+})]^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}.

So by local class field theory, the maximal pro-
\ell
abelian subextension of
K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
inside
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{pre}
has Galois group isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})]^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
, where the extra term
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
comes from the unramified part. Recall that when we define
G_{\pos}^{\#}(K)
, we quotient out a copy of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})]
that is generated by
a_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
. So we conclude that the Galois group of the maximal abelian subextension of
(K_{\pos}^{\#})_{{\mathfrak{P}}}/K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a quotient of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})]^{\frac{\ell-3}{2}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. By the Burnside basis theorem for pro-
\ell
groups, the inertia subgroup of
(K_{\pos}^{\#})_{{\mathfrak{P}}}/K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the
\Gal(K_{{\mathfrak{p}}}/Q_{v})
-closed normal subgroup generated by
\frac{\ell-3}{2}
well-chosen elements.
Finally, we look at all the primes above
v
, and obtain that the kernel of the quotient map (
6.7
) is the
\Gal(K/Q)
-closed normal subgroup generated by
\frac{\ell-3}{2}
elements. The rank of
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}^{\times}
is exactly
\frac{\ell-3}{2}
.

The construction above can be applied to other cases, for example, to the case that
Q={\mathbb{Q}}(\mu_{\ell^{m}})
and
K
is
\ell
-CM. However, the situations that can be covered are rare because of the
\ell
-CM condition. It would be interesting to understand how to generalize the definition of
\ell
-CM to cover more cases.

Appendix A
A random group model for random 3-manifolds

In the work
[
11
]
, Dunfield and Thurston studied random orientable 3-manifolds constructed by a random Heegaard splitting, which means gluing two handlebodies by a random walk in the mapping class group of a surface. They compared the probabilities that the random 3-manifolds have finite covers of particular kinds to the analogous probabilities coming from random balanced presentations, and concluded that the fundamental groups of random 3-manifolds have many more finite quotients than the random group model defined by random balanced presentations. In this section, we use the results from Section
5
to construct a random group model to simulate the fundamental groups of 3-manifolds.

Let
\Sigma_{g}
be a closed surface of genus
g
, and let
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
be its mapping class group. Then the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})
of
\Sigma_{g}
has a presentation
\langle a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},b_{1},\ldots,b_{g}\mid[a_{1},b_{1}]\cdots[a_{g},b_{g}]\rangle
, and
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
is naturally isomorphic to an index-2 subgroup of
\Out(\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}))
. For an element
\varphi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
, the 3-manifold
M
obtained by gluing two copies of
\Sigma_{g}
by
\varphi
has fundamental group

\pi_{1}(M)={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{g})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{g})]}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{g})]}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{g})]}$}}},

where
\phi\in\Aut(\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}))
is a lift of
\varphi
, and
[a_{1},\ldots,a_{g},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{g})]
means the normal subgroup of
\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})
generated by those elements. Also, the map from
\Sigma_{g}
to the classifying space
B\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})
defines a homology class in
H_{2}(\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}),{\mathbb{Z}})
, which is invariant under the action of
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
.
For a finite group
H
, Dunfield and Thurston showed that, as
g\to\infty
, the expected number of covers with covering group
H
of the random 3-manifold
M
goes to
|[H,H]|\cdot|H_{2}(H,{\mathbb{Z}})|/|\Aut(H)|
(
[
11
, Thm.6.21]
). Therefore, when we compute the number of surjections from
\pi_{1}(M)
to
H
, we have

\lim_{g\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}(\#\Sur(\pi_{1}(M),H))=|[H,H]|\cdot|H_{2}(H,{\mathbb{Z}})|.

Recall that,
H_{2}(\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}),{\mathbb{Z}})
can be identified with the kernel of a Schur covering of
\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})
. We will explicitly give a pro-
\ell
random group model that have the above moments for all
\ell
-groups
H
.

Let
\ell
be a prime number. We use the notation in Section
5
to let
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
denote the pro-
\ell
Demuškin group whose abelianization is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{2n}
and let

1\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\overset{\pi_{n}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\longrightarrow 1

denote the Schur covering of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
.
We use the presentation
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=\langle a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\mid[a_{1},b_{1}]\cdots[a_{n},b_{n}]\rangle
, and let
\xi_{n}\in H_{2}({\mathcal{G}}_{n},{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq\ker\pi_{n}
denote the image of
[a_{1},b_{1}]\cdots[a_{n},b_{n}]
.

Proposition A.1
.

Consider the random group

Z_{n}:={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\displaystyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{n})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\textstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{n})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{n})]}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{[a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\phi(a_{1}),\ldots,\phi(a_{n})]}$}}}

where
\phi
is a random automorphism of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
that preserves and acts trivially on
\ker\pi_{n}
. Then for any finite
\ell
-group
H
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\Sur(Z_{n},H)\right)=|[H,H]|\cdot|H_{2}(H,{\mathbb{Z}})|.

Proof.

For an automorphism
\phi
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}
, a surjection from
Z_{n}\to H
defines a surjection
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n}\to Z_{n}\to H
whose kernel contains
a_{i}
and
\phi(a_{i})
for all
i
. So we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\#\Sur(Z_{n},H)\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},H)\\
\text{s.t. }\pi(a_{i})=1\,\forall i\end{subarray}}\Prob\left(\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},\pi_{n};1)\,\Big|\,\phi(a_{i})\in\ker\pi,\,\forall i\right),

where
\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},\pi_{n};1)
denote the closed subgroup of
\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n})
consisting of all automorphisms that preserve and act trivially on
\ker\pi_{n}
.
Each
\pi
as described in the sum factors through a surjection
\varpi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)
because
\pi([a_{1},b_{1}]\cdots[a_{n},b_{n}])=1
.
By Lemma
5.5
,
\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},\pi_{n};1)
acts transitively on the set
\left\{\varpi\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)\mid\varpi_{*}(\xi_{n})=1\right\}
.
Since
\phi(a_{i})\in\ker\pi
if and only if
\pi\circ\phi(a_{i})=1
, we have

\Prob\left(\phi\in\Aut(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},\pi_{n};1)\,\Big|\,\phi(a_{i})\in\ker\pi,\,\forall i\right)=\frac{\#\{\rho\in\Sur(\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{n},H)\mid\rho(a_{i})=1,\,\forall i\}}{\#\{\rho\in\Sur({\mathcal{G}}_{n},H)\mid\rho_{*}(\xi_{n})=1\}}.

The numerator equals the number of surjections from a free group on generators
b_{i}
’s to
H
, so it is approximately
|H|^{n}
for large
n
. Let
F_{2n}
denote the free pro-
\ell
group on the generators
a_{1},\ldots,b_{n}
, and
\widetilde{H}\to H
is a Schur covering of
H
. Homomorphisms from
F_{2n}
to
H
lift to homomorphisms from
F_{2n}
to
\widetilde{H}
. As
n\to\infty
, considering all homomorphisms from
F_{2n}
to
H
, the image of
[a_{1},b_{1}]\cdots[a_{n},b_{n}]
is nearly uniformly distributed in
[\widetilde{H},\widetilde{H}]
. So the denominator above is approximately
|H|^{2n}/|[\widetilde{H},\widetilde{H}]|=|H|^{2n}/\left(|[H,H]|\cdot|H_{2}(H,{\mathbb{Z}})|\right)
. Therefore, we obtain

{\mathbb{E}}(\#\Sur(Z_{n},H))\sim|H|^{n}\cdot\frac{|H|^{n}}{|H|^{2n}/(|[H,H]|\cdot|H_{2}(H,Z)|)},

and the proposition follows after taking
n\to\infty
.
∎
